# Status

Owned by the coding session. Updated at the end of every coding session.

## Current

- **Milestone:** V1 fixes 2 (roadmap "V1 fixes 2": code from Galaxy Stars and smashy smashy; decisions 2026-11-22). V1 batch 2 was merged into `main` at `dc4f8ef`, and decision `95bc4ab` (questions 184-185) put smashy smashy's gaps 1 and 3 in this milestone.
- **Branch:** `v1-fixes-2`, from `main` at `95bc4ab`. One commit per item, each with its benchmark, Galaxy Stars and smashy smashy numbers in the message. The suite passes: 11,868 compiler tests on the C64 (3 skipped: `TERN_COMPARE` and the two `TERN_REAL` checks) and 50 CPU tests; `TERN_TARGET=atari`, `apple2` and `nes` with `--filter "Platform!=c64"`: 11,287 each. The benchmarks did not move with any item (the baseline is unchanged).
- **Done** (every item of the roadmap's list; `q + c as u16 + 3` was dropped from it, decision 2026-11-22, question 187):
  - Gap 33, `if c { break }` / `continue` as one branch (`8893676`), and a pass that turns any branch over a `jmp` into the opposite branch (`InvertBranchesOverJumps`).
  - Gap 30, a 16-bit compare with a constant whose low byte is 0 (`85bd3a2`), and a sum of two widened bytes (`6e64641`).
  - Smashy smashy gap 1, the "Declare 'SCREEN' as a pointer" fix where a pointer is expected (`5a0126e`); with it, a widened byte plus a constant with a low byte of 0 loads the constant's high byte.
  - Smashy smashy gap 3, `a[f()] += 1` (`1f194ca`): the index into the statement temporary first.
  - The subtract is the compare (`4e37759`): not when the other branch reads `a`, which a compare leaves in A (smashy smashy's `walk_anim` was 11 cycles a frame slower without that rule).
  - Gap 32, the loop variable passed in its own register (`11d93ce`).
  - Gap 31, a 16-bit local used for its bytes (`877ee61`): the optimizer pass `StoreWhereComputed` stores a copied byte where it was computed; the reload and the local's stores then go.
  - Platform folders (`b0af55c`; spec 2.2): `<folder>/<target>/` before `<folder>/`, a file in a platform folder imports from the folder above, another platform's folder is an error, the library-name check covers platform folders.
  - The small shapes (`be6f4f0`): `q - v` from A as `eor #$FF` / `sec` / `adc q`; temporaries and register parameters no instruction names get no memory (the zero page they free made smashy smashy 374 bytes smaller and 217 cycles a play frame faster); `r[3] = a - b` was already direct. `t = q + c as u16 + 3` is not built: dropped from the milestone (question 187).
- **Measured** (`compare.py`, all frames the same in every build): Galaxy Stars 18,750 -> 18,674 bytes, 4,706 -> 4,740 cycles per play frame (Prog8 5,586); the code only got shorter, and the 34 cycles are loop branches that now cross a page (after gap 33: `hostiles.explosion_hit`, `explode.draw_area`; after gap 31: `stars.next_busy`), found with a script over the listings. Smashy smashy 21,436 -> 20,944 bytes, 7,056 -> 6,789 cycles per play frame (Prog8 8,654). With the final build, smashy smashy's `compare.py --scenario game --frames 15000`: 3 runs, 39,620 `wait_frame()` calls the same, levels 1-9 reached in each. Galaxy Stars' plain routines measured again: none within 5% of the assembly on both the average and the worst frame, so none moves to plain Tern in batch 3; the closest is `explode.hits` with the asm's signature (2.6% / 3.0% on `check_player`, 7.3% on the worst `check_explosions` frame). Recorded in the port's `gaps.md`, section "V1 fixes 2", with two new forms in its `plain/` (`explode.hits.y`, `explode.hits.carry`), and in smashy smashy's `gaps.md`, section "V1 fixes 2"; **not committed in either game repository** (each commit there waits for the developer's approval), and `validation/c64/*/gaps.md` are not copied again yet.
- **Review** `docs/reviews/v1-fixes-2.md`: ready to merge, two suggestions, both done (table below); `main` merged in (decision `25c3ac1`, questions 186-187). The suite: 11,870 compiler tests on the C64, 11,289 on each other target.
- **Next step:** `/merge v1-fixes-2`. Then, once the developer approves: commit the two game repositories' `gaps.md` sections (and Galaxy Stars' two `plain/` files), and copy each `gaps.md` into `validation/c64/<game>/` again. Then V1 batch 3 (smashy smashy on the NES), which starts by splitting the C64 port into platform folders.
- **Tools used, not in either repository:** py65 for `compare.py` (`pip install --target <dir> py65`); this session's copy is in its scratchpad. A small script found the page-crossing branches: for each `bxx` in a listing, the page of the next instruction against the page of the target.
- **Methods over 100 lines:** none added (the longest changed: `ModuleLoader.Find` 45 lines, `GenBinaryA` 64, `SubtractIsCompare` 49).

### V1 batch 2 (merged into `main` at `dc4f8ef`)

- **Branch:** `v1-smashy`, from `main` at `9462661`. Its own commits change only the test harness, `validation/` and this file. Review `docs/reviews/v1-batch-2.md` handled (table below), `main` merged in. The suite passes: 11,747 compiler tests on the C64 (3 skipped: the `TERN_COMPARE` comparison and the two `TERN_REAL` VICE checks, which pass with `TERN_REAL=1`) and 50 CPU tests; the new tests are C64-only, so the `TERN_TARGET` runs are as before. Every item of batch 2 is done (below).
- **Done:**
  - **Galaxy Stars' three routines in plain Tern** (in the game's repository, `C:\game-dev\galaxy-star`, branch `tern-port`): `hostiles.move`, `explode.restore_area` and `title.sweep_all` replaced by their `plain/` versions; `hostiles.move_edge` and `title.COLOR_RAM_HI`, used only by the assembly, removed. `compare.py --scenario game`: all 13,211 frames the same, 18,750 bytes (was 18,682), 4,706 cycles per play frame (was 4,680); the title shows in VICE. Recorded in the port's `gaps.md` (section "V1 batch 2") and `plain/README.md`; committed there with `plain/` as `b206fc2`, after the developer approved (2026-11-22). Neither game repository is pushed: the developer has not decided whether they go online yet.
  - **Galaxy Stars in `validation/c64/galaxy-stars/`** (`5eff12f`; question 179): `src/` (the port with the change above; `video.tern`'s two `include_bytes` read `../data/`), `data/` (`chars.bin`, `sprites.bin`), `gaps.md`, `LICENSE` (MIT, the developer's), `README.md`. The build is byte for byte the port's.
  - **Its known result in the suite** (`5eff12f`): `ValidationTests.Galaxy_stars_plays_its_demo` runs it 3000 frames with idle input (title, pages, then the demo playing) and compares the screen at `$C000`, colour RAM and `$D000-$D02E` with `expected/frame-3000.txt`; `TERN_UPDATE_VALIDATION=1` rewrites it. About 6 s. Since review V1 batch 2 (finding 1) the run has `C64Chips` (the VIC's raster line and raster interrupt at the line the game sets, from the cycle count) and the KERNAL IRQ entry stub at `$FFC0` (`ValidationTests.Kernal`), since the game's sprites are in the RAM under `$EA31`. `Run.Of` takes `until`, a stop condition, for a program whose main never returns.
  - **The `TERN_REAL` check** (`a7e6d59`): `Galaxy_stars_plays_its_demo_in_vice` runs the build in VICE (`C:/Vice/bin/x64sc.exe`) for 60 million cycles and compares the screenshot with `expected/vice-60M-cycles.png`; up to 1% of the pixels may differ (provisional, question 184). `Png` (test harness) reads 8-bit RGB/RGBA PNGs with .NET's zlib, no new package.
  - **Smashy smashy ported** in the game's repository, `C:\Games\c64-platformer-demo` (the developer's "wrong folder, temporary name"), branch `tern-port` (from its `main` at `2b190d9`), folder `tern/`, the Galaxy Stars way (the developer's choice): `AGENTS.md` (the port's rules and six steps), `build.cmd`, `tools/assets.py` (writes `src/assets.tern` from the generated `../src/gen_assets.p8`; `gfx.py` unchanged), `tools/compare.py` (both builds in py65), `gaps.md`. Steps 1-5 done and committed there after the developer played each (`c342864`, `d054999`, `86b3795`, `41fe152`, `2b50e9b`): the whole game, 21,436 bytes (Prog8 25,932 with its self-tests). Checks: `compare.py --scenario levels`, 200 generated levels, every tile map and platform map the same as Prog8's, `generate` 6% fewer cycles; `--scenario play`, 40 levels, 60,000 play frames with the same random joystick input, the whole game state the same after every frame, a play frame's parts 6,845 cycles against Prog8's 8,666 (21% fewer; the Prog8 assembly `walk_anim`, `popup.draw`, `scan_row`, `find_hideout` and `title.move_rows` are plain Tern in the port); VICE: title, HOW TO PLAY and SCORING pixel for pixel Prog8's at the same cycle counts. Gaps logged in its `gaps.md` (for the spec session): 1, a message (`SCREEN + col` passed as a pointer takes two fixes); 2, `tern run` treats CIA 1 port A as plain RAM, so `--set '$DC00=...'` is lost at a program's first keyboard scan (checked with a five-line program); 3, `a[f()] += 1` is not supported though `a[f()] = g()` is. Two bugs in the Prog8 game, not Tern gaps, found by the comparisons: `nav.p8`'s `scan_row` ends in an asm block and Prog8 adds no `rts`, so it falls into `make_links` (the port does what the code means, the developer's choice; `compare.py` gives the Prog8 build its `rts` in the simulator); `pages.p8`'s `wait()` can start mid-frame and lock its raster split a frame out of step (shown by Tern's faster drawing; the port clears `frame_ready` first, as `level_start` does). Steps 4 and 5 compiled on the first try. **Step 6 done** (`295793c` there): `tools/compare_game.py` (`compare.py --scenario game`) runs both builds whole in py65 from their `SYS` addresses, with the raster line from the cycle count, a frame IRQ every PAL frame through a KERNAL IRQ entry stub at `$FFC0`, and a scripted joystick on CIA 1 port A indexed by `input.read()` calls; 3 runs of 15,000 frames (title, pages, games, levels 1-9, level complete, game over), 39,620 `wait_frame()` calls, the game state, screen, colour RAM and popup digits the same at every one. What it sets apart because the builds run at different speeds is in the port's `gaps.md`, step 6.
  - **Smashy smashy in `validation/c64/smashy-smashy/`** (`a7532fa`): `src/` (the port at `295793c` there, with the generated `assets.tern`; `video.tern`'s `include_bytes` read `../data/`), `data/`, `gaps.md`, `LICENSE` (MIT, the developer's), `README.md`; the build is byte for byte the port's. Its known result: `ValidationTests.Smashy_smashy_plays_a_scripted_game` runs 2500 frames with a scripted joystick (title, fire, play; the first game ends, the second is being played; 3000 before the review's finding 1) and compares the score, timer, level, treasures, kills and high score, the screen, colour RAM and VIC registers with `expected/frame-2500.txt` (about 5 s). The game waits for raster lines and scans the keyboard, so the run adds `C64Chips` (test harness): a bus with `$D012`/`$D011` from the cycle count and CIA 1 as the joystick and no keys; `Run.Of` takes `bus`, a wrapper for the machine's bus. Its popups use `$FF40-$FFBF`, so `ValidationTests.Kernal` puts the KERNAL IRQ entry stub at `$FFC0`, for both games since the review. `TERN_REAL=1`: `Smashy_smashy_shows_its_scoring_page_in_vice`, VICE at 25 million cycles with no input (the SCORING page, whose chain values need the raster split) against `expected/vice-25M-cycles.png`; passed twice.
- **The known results are the test harness's, not `tern run`'s** (review finding 2). Decision 179 and roadmap V1 say "in `tern run`"; both games' results need what only the harness has: a KERNAL IRQ entry (`ValidationTests.Kernal`; `tern run --irq` goes through `$FFFE` into nothing: Galaxy Stars stops with "Illegal opcode $54 at $69F2"), the VIC's raster line and raster interrupt and CIA 1 port A as a joystick ANDed with the keyboard scan (`C64Chips`), a stop after a number of frames (`Run.Of`'s `until`), and the screen at the VIC bank's address (`$C000`; `--screen` shows `$0400`, `--dump $C000:1000` works). Giving `tern run` these is out of scope (the developer, 2026-11-22: it would become a C64 emulator); question 185 asks the spec session to change the wording.

Use a T here" (a number is converted only to types with their own messages, now that a void operand is caught first). Each is a defensive check; kept, since a later change of the earlier check would reach it. After review R6 follow-up (`docs/reviews/r6-followup.md`, all three suggestions): a call with no value is one check, `RequireValue`, in `Coerce`, binary and unary operators, `as` and conditions (finding 1); a bool operand of `+ - & | ^` gets "'+' needs numbers. Use '&&', '||' and '!' for bool values", no longer in the unreachable list (finding 2); the fragment checks in `AlignTests`, `LoopCounterTypeTests` and `ModuleTests` and the `step 128` error check whole messages and positions (finding 3). Left: two arrays in an operator (`a + b`) get "Cannot combine [u8; 2] and [u8; 2]. Convert one side with 'as'", whose fix does not apply (an array does not convert); it has a row with that text, and is not changed here |

Left by hand, each choosing children on purpose: `NeverFinishes` / `Breaks` (statements, one rule since semantics 20), `NeedsInterpreter`, the interpreter's `IsConstant` and `NamesAddress` (a table read is a value), CodeGen's predictions (`UsesY`, `MaxOf`, `AssignsVar`); `Describe` (not a walk), `ConstFnBodyCheck` |

Breaking the shared scroll or read fails tests on each machine |
| tools 1 (run model split) | `ac72782` | | partly: the stand-in registry; the rest is R6 (platforms as data) |
| tests 12 (library tests against the tests' own ROM model) | `29c9ad0` | `AtariLibraryTests`, `OverlayTests`, `Apple2IoTests`, every Apple II test | partly: `Run.Program(standIns: true)` runs with the platform's `Machine`, and every Apple II run does (the hand-assembled MLI and the `rts` patches are gone); the traps that copied a stand-in (SETVBV, the dli example's CIO, the overlay tests' LOAD, the files example's COUT) are gone. ## Done

- **R5: structure, and readiness for the language server** (2026-11-16, merged as `edc9d6a`; the R5 table above has one row per finding):
  - **Milestone:** R5, structure, and readiness for the language server (roadmap "Before the first release: fixes and cleanup", R5; review themes 4 and 6 in `docs/reviews/code-quality.md`: syntax 1, 7, 8, 10, 20; semantics 18, 19; backend 12, 14; tools 7; with what earlier milestones left for R5: tools 15, syntax 21's recovery words, four small output bugs of tools 18). R1-R4 are merged.
  - **Branch:** `r5-structure`, from `main` at `824cf88` (the merge of R4); `main` merged in again after review R5.
  - **Done on the branch:** all of R5 (table below). `TernCompiler.Build` is a `Builder` with one method per job; `ParseProgram` files declarations by kind (`FileDeclarations`), with modifiers read first and no record copies; parser modes are scopes (`Mode<T>`); the checker's body state is one `BodyContext`, and `Run`, `CheckExpr`, `AddEffects` and `MemoryLayout.Build` are split into named steps; CodeGen's per-function state is one `FunctionContext`, and `Gen16`, `GenForLoop` and `GenAssign` are split; `Runner.Run` has `Start` and `CallTimes` apart; after review R5, `Assembler.Assemble` (an `Assembly` per call, `Assembler.Layout.cs`) and `Allocator.Allocate` are split too. For M28: tokens have end offsets and keep `///` comments, nodes have a `Span`, top-level declarations keep their doc comment and are listed in source order (`ProgramSyntax.Declarations`), and `TernCompiler.Check` checks without building and returns its errors (`CheckResult`), reading modules open in an editor from their text. The bus takes cycles through `Cpu6502.Stall`, and the CPU attaches itself to its bus. 6119 compiler tests and 50 CPU tests pass, none skipped. No benchmark moved (1254 / 350780), and all 38 examples build byte for byte as on `main` (checked after the review's fixes).
  - **Behavior changes** (each with a test that failed before it): `tern run`'s one-byte dump says "(1 byte)" (`SkillTests` rewritten: two dumps in `language.md` and `sprite.md`, nothing else moved); an index error with no line prints `file: message`, not `file::`; `LineTable.Format` of no rows no longer throws; the Apple II's MLI stand-in wraps its return address at `$01FF`; `Cpu6502.Step` returns the cycles of an OAM DMA too (the totals were and are right). Error recovery: the statement column is now restored after an error in a block (syntax 20), and a top-level declaration checked on demand starts with a fresh body state (semantics 18). Neither can be seen in a program (review R5, finding 3): an error leaves a block early only at the end of the file, after which nothing is parsed, or with no error list, where parsing stops; and the declarations checked on demand while a body is checked are constants, whose values take no address, call nothing and use no loop register, so the old body state had nothing to act on.
  - **Review R5:** finding 1 (must fix): `Assembler.Assemble` and `Allocator.Allocate` were neither split nor listed; the status said wrongly that they had been split earlier (the session's method count missed signatures over several lines). Split (`745484d`, `3979e7e`), the backend 12 row corrected. Finding 2: `Check`'s and `CheckResult.Ok`'s summaries now say that allocation, code generation and layout errors come from `Compile` only, with the review's zero-page case in `CheckTests`. Finding 3: not observable (above). Finding 4: the small things fixed; an `Error` token starts at the error's place; the `///` rule is question 176.
  - **Done when:** met as far as the coding session can tell: methods follow the method-length convention (none over 300 lines; the ones over 100 are listed under Known limitations, with the reason), the check-only entry has tests (`CheckTests`), and the benchmarks are unchanged. `SkillTests` changed only by the "(1 byte)" fix, which status gave R5 (tools 18).

- **R2: fixes in the backend and the checker** (2026-11-15, roadmap R2, reviews `code-quality-backend.md` and `code-quality-semantics.md`; the table above has one row per finding):

  - *Checker* (`af2fa27`, `ab18427`, `221cdae`, `34e10f8`, `af84911`, `4d088d8`, `573d381`, `2f48182`, `94a816f`): the `for` step is lowered and walked by `ContainsCall` (`ForStmt.Step` is settable); `DataValue` reads a single `data` value at compile time; main-file interrupt fns no root reaches are walked for recursion; constant typing (a typed constant narrows only a value that fits; `as` keeps the low bits of any constant; `.lo`/`.hi` of an untyped constant are its two bytes); the loop counter's type from the values it takes (`CounterType`; `StepByOne` ends `0..256` with `inx` / `bne`, `StepGeneral` ends a u8 loop with an end past 255 on the carry); `ResolveType` passes the long-array and list-length rules to the outermost type only; data in a zero-page-only asm mode is an error; an undeclared name in a `using` loop is not counted as a call; type and asm messages with fix sentences (`FixSentenceTests` has one exemption fewer); `@ name` in a module uses the main file's regions and `chr` areas, and a module symbol of that name is an error.
  - *Tests*: R1's marked theories now run with the others (the matrix's known-failure list is gone; `VolatileAccessTests` keeps one for question 170). New: `LoopCounterTypeTests`, `OperandMatrix16Tests` (912 cases), and the tests named in the table. Conversions of constants (`as`, `.lo`/`.hi`) now have tests, part of R1's tests 10; precedence tests are still missing.

- **R1: tests that find bugs where features meet** (2026-11-15, roadmap R1, review `code-quality-tests.md`):
  - *Random start* (`669124d`, finding 2): `StartState` fills all memory with bytes from a fixed seed (6502) before the program is loaded, and sets A, X, Y non-zero and the carry, V and N; used by `Run` and `Library`. `Run.Program(..., zeroStart: true)` gives the old state. Seventeen tests read memory nothing had written (stand-in buffers and counters, fixed-address variables, bytes past a load) and now set it in their setup; none was a compiler bug.
  - *Bus log* (`1ea03d9`, finding 3): `BusLog` wraps the bus in `Run.Program(..., watch: result => addresses)` and logs the CPU's reads and writes of those addresses; `Writes` leaves out an NMOS read-modify-write's write-back of the old value. `VolatileTests`, `Apple2Tests`, `AtariTests` and `IndexedOperandTests` count volatile reads and writes on the bus instead of in the listing. `VolatileAccessTests`: 27 statements into a volatile variable, array element and struct field at 8 and 16 bits, each checked for the one value written per byte and the reads, and reads of a volatile source.
  - *Optimized against plain* (`d061f7d`, finding 8): `TernCompiler.Compile(..., optimize: false)` skips `Optimizer.Run` (functions and interrupt handlers; not in the CLI). Every `Run.Program` with no setup, traps, IRQs or buttons is repeated without the optimizer, and every global (not pointers) must end with the same value; programs with `addr(` or `noinit` are left out, since addresses and noinit memory differ with the layout. `comparePlain: false` turns it off for one run (used once, for an asm block that stores an address). About 60% of runs are compared.
  - *Operand matrix* (`53ae764`, `1da18e0`, finding 1): `OperandMatrixTests` puts 12 operand forms (global, zero page, local, parameter, field at offset 1, element of a linear and of an indexed struct array, array element, pointer target, a call that changes X and Y, volatile, constant) in 24 places (for start, end and step, match, indexed match, index read and write, arguments, conditions, operators, while, pointer store, assignment targets, return, return into the carry), once and in `using X` / `using Y` loops that start at 1, against a C# model; forms a place does not allow must give an error (calls in a `using` loop, a pointer access in a `using Y` loop, writes to a call or constant, constants out of range). 1067 cases pass in about 3 seconds. `EvaluationOrderTests` adds `for` bounds and steps and returned compares.
  - *One listing reader* (`7e9d042`, `408b339`, finding 7): `Listing` (tests) reads a function's instructions from `CompileResult.Instructions` by section, so inlined code belongs to its caller and a function ends where its section does (the copies disagreed on this); `Lines`, `Annotated` (with the function's labels and source comments), `CodeWithLabels` and `Count`. About 30 per-file readers are gone; no test cuts instructions out of listing text, and no expected string changed.
  - Review fixes: the random start in `BinaryTests`, `SpriteTests`, the raster example and the Atari VBI tests (`55322c6`; the music player test now clears the SID registers it samples before the player writes them); the raster test uses `BusLog`; the matrix's error cases check a part of the message (`0f9a8ef`).
  - Not in R1 (left for R4-R6 as the roadmap says): scoping the 91 whole-listing `Assert.Contains` (finding 9), full-text error checks (4), the feature suite on every platform (5), the shared `TempFolder` and `Cli.Tern` helpers (7).

Suggestion 8 is question 169.

- **M27: NES** (2026-11-14, branch `m27-nes`; decisions 2026-11-14).
  - *Apple II prefix* (`d391915`, question 150 as settled): the startup's prefix code (53 bytes) only when the program imports `io`; the report has "io: 53 bytes of startup code, which set an empty prefix to the program's folder". Tests in `Apple2IoTests`.
  - *The cartridge line and the NES target* (`0efefbe`): `cartridge board option...` in the memory block (`Board`, `Cartridge` in `Cartridge.cs`), boards `nrom` and `uxrom`, mirroring `vertical` or `horizontal`, required on the NES (also with no memory block), an error elsewhere (C64, Atari: not supported yet; Apple II: no cartridges). `Platform.Nes`: `ricoh2a03`, zero page `$00-$FF`, `VariableRam` `$0200-$07FF` (`MemoryLayout.Variables`; the assembler's `variableAreas` puts the variables' block there), volatile `$2000-$401F`, no default encoding (`Platform.DefaultEncoding` is nullable; an unprefixed literal without `default encoding` is an error), `OutputFormat.Nes`. NROM's code areas are `$C000-$FFFF` then `$8000-$BFFF`, so a program that fits in 16 KB is NROM-128 (seen at both addresses), a larger one NROM-256.
  - *ROM and RAM*: on a cartridge, variables with an initial value are in the RAM block (`RamPlan.CopiedGlobals`) and their values a table in ROM ("initial values") that the startup copies; `data` stays in ROM; `data` outside ROM is an error. Aligned variables go first in the block (RAM is cleared once, so the zeroed ones need not be one block).
  - *Startup* (`TernCompiler.Nes.cs`): `sei`, `cld`, `$4017` = `$40`, stack, `$2000`, `$2001` and `$4010` = 0, on UxROM bank 0 (`stx bank_zero`, a 0 in the fixed bank, for the bus conflict), `bit $2002`, two vblank waits with `$0000-$07FF` cleared between, zero-page initial values, the copy, `jsr main`, `jmp halt`. Zeros are not stored again.
  - *Vectors*: reset is the compiler's; `data` at `$FFFA`/`$FFFE` must be `addr()` of an `interrupt fn` (not a part) or an `asm fn`; unset vectors point to an `rti` after `halt`; `data` over `$FFFC` is an error. A single value of `data` may be declared when placed (question 157). Placed `data` is kept in every module, with the functions it names (`Checker.KeptGlobals`).
  - *Output*: iNES 1.0 (`.nes`): header, PRG (unused bytes `$FF`), CHR ROM (8 KB on NROM) or none (CHR RAM); Mesen labels (`.mlb`, `LabelFormat.Mesen`: `NesPrgRom:offset:name`, `NesInternalRam:address:name`, dots as `_`), which Mesen 2.1.1 loads by itself (checked with `emu.getLabelAddress`). Report: "Cartridge: ...", "Variables in RAM: ...", bank and chr areas.
  - *`chr` areas and `@ area + offset`* (`18c0f54`): `$0000..=$1FFF chr tiles` (PPU addresses, no CPU memory; NROM only, one line); only `data` there, its bytes in CHR ROM; code may use `addr(x)` (a `u16`) and `len(x)` only. `@ name + offset` on regions and chr areas on every target (`VariableSymbol.RegionOffset`); errors: offset past the end, overlapping declarations (question 165).
  - *`tern run`* (`NesBus`, `NesMachine`; `0efefbe`, `935b7c6`): RAM mirrored, PRG ROM copied into `Ram` at `$8000-$FFFF` (a UxROM latch write copies the selected bank, with the bus conflict), PPU memory through `$2006`/`$2007` (16 KB, nametable mirroring, palette mirrors, increment 1 or 32, buffered reads), OAM through `$2003`/`$2004` and DMA `$4014` (513 cycles), `$2002` vblank set each 29,781 cycles and cleared by a read, an NMI with bit 7 of `$2000`, scroll registers, controllers from `NesBus.Buttons` (tests; `tern run` has no buttons). `--screen` shows nametable 0 through the program's encoding (`CompileResult.TextEncoding`). The tests' `Run.Program` takes `buttons` and exposes `Nes`.
  - *`lib/nes`* (`nes.tern`, `uxrom.tern`): spec 12.2's list. The PPUDATA field is `ppu.value`, since `data` is a keyword (question 167). `uxrom.select(n)` is `tax` / `sta BANKS,x` over a table of 0-15; on a cartridge assembly may store to `data` (question 159). Importing `uxrom` on another board is an error.
  - *Banks* (`b5d0a0d`, `0c53145`): `$8000..=$BFFF bank name` (UxROM only, at the board's window), `import m @ area[n]` (`ImportDecl.Bank`, `Module.Bank`, `Module.Unit`: an overlay module or `area[n]`). The rules between overlays of one area compare units, so calls, data uses and addresses between banks are errors; bank modules' variables are resident. Errors: no number, a number on an overlay area, not constant, out of range, the fixed bank's. The image: banks by number, as many as the highest needs with the fixed bank, a power of two, the fixed bank last; a bank that does not fit lists its modules. `implements` works on banks. `bank` is no longer a reserved word (question 164). An import that places a module in a bank is not warned as unused (question 163).
  - *Examples and Mesen* (`2169ede`): `examples/nes/hello.tern` (NROM, a font in a chr area, text) and `examples/nes/scroll.tern` with `art.tern` (bank 0) and `motion.tern` (bank 1) on UxROM: the done-when example. In Mesen 2.1.1 (test runner, screenshots at frames 60, 150, 300 and 301): the starry sky scrolls over the brick ground and four balls bounce, also across the nametable switch at 256 pixels; hello shows "HELLO NES!". `tests/tools/mesen-shot.ps1 game.nes shot.png <frames...>` writes the Lua script and saves the screenshots.
  - *Tests*: `NesTests` (37), `NesLibraryTests` (14), `NesBankTests` (18), region offsets in `MemoryTests`, the examples in `RepositoryWarningTests`.
  - *Skill* (`9537abf`): the NES in `SKILL.md`, banks, `@ region + offset` and the `nes`/`uxrom` modules in `language.md`.

- **M26 follow-up review** (2026-11-13, `docs/reviews/m26-followup.md`): not ready to merge; three findings, two suggestions, all done (`ce39ec9`).
  - Finding 1: the variables' block took its modulus from every global in RAM, also aligned `data` and initialized variables, which are aligned in the file, so `data font ... @ align(2048)` moved the block to the next multiple of 2048. Now only the block's own variables count. Test `AlignTests.Aligned_data_does_not_move_the_variables_after_the_program`.
  - Finding 2: the crash in the block's alignment error (no aligned variable to name) cannot happen any more after finding 1: the error is reached only when the block's modulus comes from its own aligned variables. Test `Aligned_data_with_only_frames_after_the_program_compiles` (the reviewer's program).
  - Finding 3: `@ zp align(2)`, `@ $C000 align(2)`, `@ region align(2)`, `@ align(2) zp` and `@ align(2) region` give "'@' takes one location (an address, a region, 'zp' or 'align(n)'), and this has two. Remove 'align(n)', or the other location". Test `Align_with_another_location_is_an_error`.
  - Suggestion 4: `align(n)` as a struct field's `@` gives "A struct field's '@' is its offset from the start of the struct, so it cannot be aligned. Write the offset, as in '@ 2'". Test `A_struct_field_cannot_be_aligned`.
  - Suggestion 5: `tern run`'s OPEN returns `$42` (too many files open) when 8 files are open. Test `Apple2IoTests.Tern_run_fails_a_ninth_open_file_with_too_many_files`.

- **M26 follow-up: the Apple II's `io` and `@ align(n)`** (2026-11-13, branch `m26-apple2-io`).
  - *`@ align(n)`* (`478c2e6`, `8e5b576`; spec 4): `align` is a word only after `@`; `n` a constant power of two from 2 to 32768; on global `var` and `data`, not on locals. The variables after the program are now: aligned `noinit` globals, the zeroed globals (cleared from `RamPlan.ZeroedStart`), the other `noinit` globals, then frames. Aligned variables are placed like page-placed arrays, at a multiple of their alignment, and the block starts at a multiple of the largest alignment (`Reserve` takes a modulus and residue instead of a page offset; with page arrays the modulus is at least 256). So the block has no gaps of its own, and the padding before it is less than the largest alignment. Data, initialized variables and an overlay's variables get zero padding in their file (`Padding`, combined with the `jmp (...)` vector padding). An aligned variable never goes to zero page. The report has `Aligned name: $addr (@ align(n)), N bytes of padding before it`; the listing names the padding. Errors: `n` not a power of two in range or not constant, a local, a dereferenced pointer (question 155), `@ align` without parentheses, variables that fit no program area at their alignment ("Make a program area larger, or use a smaller alignment"), and data that fits nowhere (the usual no-room error). Programs without `@ align` are unchanged. `AlignTests` (28).
  - *`lib/apple2/io`* (`cfede86`; spec 10.1): `open` (`READ` 1, `WRITE` 2: CREATE as BIN, aux 0, access `$C3`, ignoring "duplicate"; OPEN; SET_EOF 0), `close` (no call when nothing is open, since CLOSE with reference 0 closes every file; the file is kept when CLOSE fails), `read` (sets `ST_EOF` itself when fewer bytes came: the MLI gives `$4C` only when it read nothing), `write`, `status()`, `load`, `save` (after the close, GET_FILE_INFO, then SET_FILE_INFO on the same list with type BIN and aux type `src`), `load_bin` (GET_FILE_INFO; not BIN or aux type 0 fails with `$4A`; OPEN, GET_EOF, then a read of exactly the file's length; 64 KB or more fails with `$4D`). The MLI's parameter lists are private structs; the name is copied into a private 65-byte path (length, then characters with bit 7 cleared, cut at 64); the buffer is `private noinit var buffer: [u8; 1024] @ align(256)`. `Apple2IoTests` runs it against an MLI and disk in C# (`FakeMli`): names, the buffer, modes, counts, end of file, errors, whole files, `load_bin`, the example.
  - *Prefix* (`cfede86`, `36775c0`; spec 12.2): at the end of the startup code, GET_PREFIX into `$0200`; when it is empty, the folder of the path at `$0280` (up to its last `/`, bit 7 cleared) becomes the prefix through SET_PREFIX; a partial path leaves it empty. 53 bytes in every Apple II program (hello: 471 to 524 bytes). `Run.Program` puts an MLI stub at `$BF00` (success, past the 3 bytes); tests of the four cases.
  - *`tern run`* (`cfede86`, `7d6686a`): MLI stand-ins for GET_PREFIX (empty), SET_PREFIX, and GET_FILE_INFO, OPEN, GET_EOF, READ and CLOSE on the build's overlay files, found by the path's last part upshifted, as the MLI does (`LEVEL1.BIN`, `level1.bin`, `/DISK/LEVEL1.BIN`); a name ProDOS cannot store gives `$40`, another `$46`. CREATE, DESTROY, RENAME, SET_FILE_INFO, WRITE and SET_EOF stop the run ("which writes to the disk"). GET_EOF is one more than the roadmap's list, since `load_bin` reads exactly the file's length.
  - *Disk script* (`8e5b576`, `36775c0`): `apple2-disk.ps1 program.system disk.po level1.bin@$6000 ...` adds each `.bin` as a BIN file with that address as its aux type (cp2's `#06xxxx` suffix), named in capitals. The address is given on the command line: the `.bin` has no header, and an overlay's load address is its area's first address, which the report prints.
  - *Example* (`8e5b576`): `examples/apple2/files.tern` with its overlay `greet.tern`: saves `SCORES`, loads it back, prints it in hex, loads `GREET.BIN` with `io.load_bin` and calls it.
  - *AppleWin checks* (1.32.1.0, enhanced IIe and II+ with a language card): the files example shows "SAVED SCORES", "LOADED 0A 14 1E 28 FF 00 C8 9B", "SAME BYTES", "HELLO FROM AN OVERLAY" on both; the disk then holds `SCORES`, a BIN file of 8 bytes with the address of `scores` as its aux type. `tests/tools/apple2-prefix-check.tern`: at boot ProDOS passes a partial path (`APPLE2PR.SYSTEM`) and has set the prefix (`/PRODOS.2.4.3/`); from Bitsy Bye the path is full with bit 7 set (`/` is `$AF`), and the prefix is set too, also for a program built without the prefix code (question 150). A lowercase name (`lc.test`) saves and loads, stored as `LC.TEST`. 80 columns (question 142): from Bitsy Bye, BASIC.SYSTEM, `PR#3` (an 80-column CATALOG), then `-ZHELLO.SYSTEM`: the hello example shows in 40 columns as it should, on the enhanced IIe. The screen does not show whether the startup's Ctrl-U path (CSWH `$C3`) ran or something else turned 80 columns off.
  - AppleWin notes: Bitsy Bye jumps by first letter only (`B` picks `BITSY.BOOT` before `BASIC.SYSTEM`; arrow keys sent with `SendKeys` do nothing), so a test disk deletes `BITSY.BOOT`. The Apple keyboard holds one key: `SendKeys` of a whole word loses characters, so send one key at a time with a pause (150 ms works). `dotnet test` does not rebuild `Tern.Cli`: run `dotnet build` before `dotnet run --no-build`.
  - *Skill* (`73d43cb`): `@ align(n)`, the Apple II's `io`, `tern run`'s file stand-ins, the disk script's `.bin` arguments.

- **M26 re-review** (2026-11-13, `docs/reviews/m26.md`): not ready to merge, finding 8: a variable the program requires in zero page (here a dereferenced pointer) could still be pushed onto the `$FF`/`$00` gap by the compiler's own choices placed before it. Now the least used variable the compiler chose and placed before it goes to RAM, and the rest are placed again; the error comes only when no such variable is left. Test `Apple2Tests.A_variable_required_in_zero_page_moves_one_the_compiler_chose_to_ram` (175 `u8` globals, then a dereferenced pointer).

Overlays and binaries are `.bin` (`Platform.BinaryExtension`, question 141).
  - *System file and startup* (`TernCompiler.Apple2.cs`): the file is the run of bytes that starts at `start`, then the other runs by address, with no gaps. When a run is not where the file puts it, the startup code begins with one copy per run (pointers at `$50-$53`, only relative branches, since it runs from the file at `$2000`; a run that moves up is copied from its end), then `jmp moved`. Assembly repeats until the copies agree with the layout (`SystemFile`). Errors: a copy over the copying code ("Start the first program area at $2000, or leave ... out of the program areas"), no order of copies that leaves the runs not yet copied intact, a file past `$BEFF`. Then: Ctrl-U through COUT when CSWH is `$C3`, SETNORM, INIT, SETVID, SETKBD, HOME, and the reset vector (`$3F2` and the power-up byte) to `quit`, which closes all files (MLI CLOSE, reference 0), increments `$3F4` and makes the MLI QUIT call (questions 142, 144). `CompileResult.Entry` is `$2000`. The report adds "System file: N bytes, loaded at ...; the startup code copies ... into place".
  - *`tern run`* (`Apple2Machine.cs`): text page 1 at `$0400` in the Apple II's row order (`Machine.RowAddress`); stand-ins for COUT (as COUT1: INVFLG mask from `$A0`, codes below `$80` stored as they are, return, line feed, backspace, wrap and scroll, written through BASL), HOME, SETNORM, INIT, SETVID, SETKBD, and the MLI's CLOSE (succeeds and returns after the 3 bytes) and QUIT (ends the run: "the program quit (MLI QUIT)"); other MLI calls stop the run (question 146). `Runner` and the tests' `Run.Program` load the system file at `$2000`; `Run.Program` puts an `rts` at the startup's ROM calls.

All on IOCB 1 (`io.IOCB`). `open` copies the name into a local 40-byte buffer and ends it with `$9B`. A failed open leaves the IOCB taken on a real Atari, so `load`, `save` and `load_xex` always close and keep the first error for `status()`. `load_xex` reads `$FF $FF`, then segments (an extra `$FF $FF` before one is skipped) to the end of the file; nothing is run. `AtariIoTests` (24 tests) runs it against a CIO and disk in C#.

Opening for writing, and other commands, stop the run with a message. `AtariRunTests` covers `load_xex` of an overlay, wrong names, `read` to the end, and a write stopping the run.

Without `greet.xex`: "Load failed: $AA".

A condition that is not a bool: see the review below. In runtime code (a function body, also nested blocks) it is "An assert is checked at compile time. Write it at the top level or in a const fn" (was "put it outside the function"). The warnings pass reads the condition's names.

The message is the spec's; it has no fix sentence, so `tern --json` gives `suggestion: null` for it.

(Not in `errors.md`: its examples are compiled by `SkillTests`, and this error names the library's path, which differs per machine.)

- **M24: floats as a library** (2026-11-13, branch `m24-floats`).
  - *Language* (`6e19125`): a struct literal's array field takes an array from a `const fn`, `repeat()` or a list built with `for` (spec 3.3), checked by `ArrayBytes` like a table (each element against the field's element type); a `[u16; n]` field is split into low and high bytes inside the record. A wrong length: "The field 't' is [u8; 4]: expected 4 values, found an array of 3. Return an array of 4 values". Tests in `StructLiteralTests` (a const fn, `repeat`, a list with `for`, `data` of structs, a `[u16; 2]` field; a wrong length, an element out of range, a real element).
  - *`lib/float`* (`cea0054`, `src/lib/float.tern`): `struct F32 { bytes: [u8; 4] }`, `var overflow: bool`, and every function of the 10.1 table, `of` included. The value is `±M * 2^(E - 152)`, M the 24-bit mantissa with its leading 1, so 0.1mmm × 2^(E - $80) as in Microsoft BASIC; the spec's range 2.9e-39 to 1.7e38 fixes this reading (question 126). Each public routine copies its pointers to the private zero-page `pa`/`pb`, then `jsr`s shared steps: `unpack_a`/`unpack_b` (the mantissa with its 1 and a guard byte, a 16-bit exponent, the sign), `normalize`, `finish` (rounds on the guard byte's top bit, then 0 below exponent 1, the largest value and `overflow` above 255, else stores).
  - *Rounding*: every result is the exact result rounded once, halves away from zero. `add`/`sub` swap so A is the larger magnitude and shift B with the bits past the guard byte ORed into its lowest bit (a sticky bit, which a subtraction needs; the guard is still one byte); `mul` keeps the whole 48-bit product (shift-and-add, 24 steps); `div` takes 32 quotient bits (a tie cannot happen in a division). `neg` leaves 0 as 0; `cmp` counts any value with exponent 0 as 0. `trunc` and `floor` clear the fraction bits (`chop`); `floor` of a negative value with a fraction adds 1 to the magnitude at the bit above it. `to_`/`round_` share `int_part`.
  - *`dec`* (in Tern, with the asm `to_digits`): the integer round(|x| * 10^d), or |x| shifted left when it has no fraction bits (then d zeros are written after the point), in up to 16 bytes, converted to packed decimal by doubling in decimal mode over only the bytes in use; a `-` when the text is not all zeros; at least one digit before the point; `decimals` above 9 is written as 9 (question 127).
  - *`float.of`*: finds the exponent with `round(log2(a))` and two correcting loops (the module's own `floor` hides the built-in in its const fns, question 130), then `round(a * pow(2.0, 24 - e))`. Too large: an index out of range on purpose, at a line whose comment says why: "float.tern:NN: Index 1 is outside the array (length 1) (called from main.tern:3)" (question 128).
  - *Model and tests* (`FloatModel.cs`, `FloatTests.cs`, 27 tests): the model computes each operation as an exact fraction (`BigInteger`) rounded once, so it does not repeat the 6502 algorithm. Bit for bit, with `overflow` and with q unchanged: add, sub, mul and div over all pairs of 71 values (0, ±1, ±0.5, the smallest and largest values with both signs, 1 ± an ulp, integers near the conversion limits, 2^23, random values, values near 1) and 1500 random pairs each with close exponents; ties (1 + half an ulp, 1 + 1.5 ulp, 1 - 2^-25, 4097 * 4097, a subtraction that only the sticky bit keeps below the half); overflow, division by zero (also 0 / 0), results that become 0, and the flag staying set; `neg`, `abs`, `copy`, `is_zero`, `cmp` (also a 0 with its sign bit set); `from_u16`/`from_i16` over about 2,000 values; `to_`/`round_` at 0, -1, ±0.5, halves, 32767, -32768, 65535 and out of range; `trunc`/`floor` on negative values with and without a fraction, and at 2^23 and above; `dec` at 0-10 decimals over about 100 values, and examples; `float.of` for 32 reals (ties at the smallest and at the largest value) and its error. `Cycle_counts` pins the best and worst cycles of each routine.
  - *Example* (`examples/floats.tern`): a ball thrown up at 12.5 m/s, its height and speed every quarter second in steps of 1/20 s, then 1/3, pi * 2.5 * 2.5 and round(-2.5). `Example_prints_computed_values` compares the screen with the same steps done by the model; checked in VICE.
  - *Skill*: `float` in the library table of `language.md` and its costs in `costs.md`; the struct literal's array from a `const fn`.

Its note (a constant used in an encoding's byte that needs that encoding gave "'H' depends on itself: H -> H"): `Resolve` puts the encoding on `_declaring` while it is checked, so the chain names it, "H -> encoding tiles -> H" (the rule that lets a global be demanded while a constant is worked out ignores encodings). Two tests.

Write the byte as a number", or "... uses a character without a prefix, which is in the default encoding 'tiles', and that needs this byte. Write the byte as a number, or write a prefix before the quote" (`ByteUsesDefault`, the same whichever is checked first). Tests: the review's program (one error), with no data first, the module variant, a prefixed self-reference, two encodings' bytes using each other, a base through a byte, a range under a base, and a byte using another encoding under a default (compiles).

- **Encodings declared by the program** (2026-11-12, branch `encodings`). Four commits.
  - *Question 123* (`d3df530`): an interface module placed in an overlay area (the implementing module's or another) is an error at the name after `implements`: "'api' is placed in the overlay area 'lv'; an interface must be resident. Remove '@ lv' from its import". Two tests in `ImplementsTests`.
  - *Syntax* (`6b89c85`): `encoding name [: base] { 'c' = byte, 'a'..='z' = byte }` (lines separated by `,` or a line break), `private encoding`, `default encoding name` (`EncodingDecl`, `DefaultEncoding`, `ProgramSyntax.Encodings` / `DefaultEncodings`). `encoding` is a word only before a name at the top level, `default` only before `encoding` (`IsEncodingWord`, `IsDefaultEncoding`); both stay usable as names. The lexer reads `font.tiles"HI"` as one literal with the prefix `font.tiles`. Parse errors: `..` for `..=`, a range that goes down, a prefixed character (`screen'A' = 1`), `'\0'` or `'\$NN'` as a character, a block not closed.
  - *Checks* (`Semantics/Checker.Encodings.cs`): encodings are declared with the const fns, before anything else, in the module's scope (so a name clash with any other declaration is "already declared"), then each is checked once (`Resolve`), when a literal first needs it or at its module's turn: the base (built-in or declared, in any order; `a: b`, `b: a` is an error), each byte a constant 0-255, a character listed twice, a range whose bytes pass `$FF`. A declared encoding is `CharEncoding.Declared`: its own codes, then the base's. `EncodingOf` (all literals: data, expressions, asm operands, const fns through `ICompileTimeHost.Encode`, now run in the literal's module) uses `NamedEncoding` for a prefix and `DefaultEncodingFor` otherwise: the main file's `default encoding` for the main file and program modules, the platform's for library modules (`Module.IsLibrary`). Errors: a built-in's name, an unknown encoding (the list includes the file's own), a name that is not an encoding, a module's private encoding, a missing import, a second `default encoding` line, one outside the main file. A failed encoding or default adds no errors at its uses.
  - *Warnings*: an unused encoding of the main file, or a private one of a module, is warned like other declarations; a prefix (also in a const fn body) or the default line counts as a use, and of its import. Fixed on the way: `FindWarnings` walked const fn bodies after checking unused declarations, so a constant used only in a const fn that is never called was warned (spec 13: a use in an unused function counts). The walk now comes first.
  - *Example* (`59b992c`): `examples/hudfont.tern` (20 glyphs and `encoding hud`, in the game's order) and `examples/statusline.tern` (copies the glyphs to `$2000`, sets `$D018`, writes "SCORE 01250" and three red hearts with `default encoding hudfont.hud`). Checked in `tern run` (test) and in VICE.
  - *Skill*: `language.md` section 1 has a bullet on declared encodings.
  - Tests: `EncodingTests` (50): bytes of ranges, single characters, non-ASCII characters (`♥`, `é`), a built-in base with changes, a declared base declared later with its own base, shared bytes, escapes, a constant as the byte and a constant using the encoding before the memory block, a const fn, an asm operand, `encoding`/`default` as names, a module's encoding and private one, a module's encoding as base and as default, the default in the main file and a program module but not a library module, a prefix over the default, each error with its position, one error per broken encoding, warnings; the example. `WarningTests`: a constant used only in an uncalled const fn.

Use 'header'" (decision 120, spec 8.5). `CheckImplements` records each match by module and variable (`_moduleData`); `CheckNotBinaryData`, called from `UseGlobal`, which every use goes through, reports it when the file being checked is that module. Resident code and the interface's own functions keep naming it. Tests: a read with an index, a write, `addr()`, an asm operand (each at the name), and resident code plus an interface helper called from main and from a level.

- **Overlay interfaces: `implements`** (2026-11-12, branch `implements`). Five commits.
  - *Syntax* (`70aa3e3`): `implements` is a keyword; `import m @ area implements a, b` fills `ImportDecl.Implements`.
  - *Checks* (`Semantics/Checker.Implements.cs`): for each interface, `Implement` matches every routine at a fixed address of the interface module to the module's function of the same name, as `MatchInterface` does for `export level_api` (12.3): same register parameters and return (`Signature.Matches`), not inline or interrupt (`CheckExportable`), no fixed inputs, routines 3 bytes apart and inside the module's area; each `volatile` fixed variable of the interface needs `data` of the same name, type and address in the module, inside the area. `setup` is not required. Errors: `implements` on a module not placed in an overlay (also on a module's import), an interface the main file does not import, a name given twice, an interface with no routines at a fixed address, tables of two interfaces overlapping. Each match is an `Implementation` in `CheckedProgram.Implementations`.
  - *Call graph*: each implementation is a callee of its routine (`routine.Callees`), so a call to `level_api.update()` reaches `level1.update` and `level2.update`: frames, roots, the two-roots report and recursion (`level1.update` calling `level_api.update`) all follow. An implementation that no call reaches is still compiled (`OrderRoots` adds it as an address-taken function; `MarkParts` visits it). `Allocator.FrameOffsets` placed a routine at offset 0 (it has no frame); one with implementations now follows its callers already placed (`031f193`), else the implementations shared memory with the function calling the interface. Callers from another root come later, but then the implementations have two roots and are placed last.
  - *Warnings*: the implementing import and the interface import count as used; the implementing functions and data count as named. The matched data is always in the overlay's file (`_tableData`), even when no code names it.
  - *Backend* (`TernCompiler.Build`): one fixed section per entry, named `level_api.update`, at the routine's address in the module's overlay (`SectionStart(..., Overlay: module)`), holding `jmp level1.update`; the assembler places the module's code around it, as around fixed data. Fixed data of the module over an entry gets the assembler's overlap error. After code generation, `RegisterUse` checks that no implementation changes a register its routine `keeps`.
  - *`tern run`* (`c4a529a`): stand-ins for SETNAM, SETLFS and LOAD load the build's own overlay files, the one whose module name in capitals is the file name; another name fails with carry set; secondary address 0 loads to X/Y. Before, a program with overlays could not be run. The ROM stop message lists the new stand-ins.
  - *Example*: `examples/stages.tern` with `stage_api.tern`, `stage1.tern`, `stage2.tern`: `play()` calls only `stage_api` (three entries and a title every stage provides as data at `$6009`); stages loaded in turn and stage1 again. Checked in `tern run` (a test) and in VICE (screen: "FOREST 5" over trees, "LAKE 7" over water).
  - *Skill*: `language.md` section 12 has a bullet on overlays and `implements`; `SKILL.md` says `c64.load` works in `tern run`.
  - Tests: `ImplementsTests` (38): calls through the interface reach the loaded overlay, the table's bytes, entries with gaps and code around them, a plain call next to an interface call, an uncalled interface, frames shared with other code and not with the caller, a function called from an implementation and main, the two-roots report, recursion, volatile data, two interfaces, `keeps` relied on in a `using X` loop, the example, each error. `RunToolTests` (3 more): LOAD by name, a missing file, secondary address 0.

Plus the listing of the example.

- **Warnings** (2026-11-12, branch `warnings`). Five commits.
  - *The analysis* (`1bb7364`, `Semantics/Checker.Warnings.cs`). `Lookup` records every top-level symbol it resolves (`_named`; a generator's bound names too), and each file's imports it names something of (`_usedImports`). Main-file names used in module parameter values (checked in a copy of the main file) are kept by name. After the checks find no error, `FindWarnings`:
    - unused locals: `LocalUses` walks each function body (a local, local `const`, `for` variable, asm fn `var` line, const fn local); an assignment target is a write, not a read (a pointer stored through is read); `for` variables get "Write '_' as the loop variable". A const fn's body is never resolved by the checks, so the walk also records its top-level names and imports.
    - unused declarations: consts, globals, data, functions, const fns and structs of the main file, `private` ones of modules; not `main`, exports (also the functions matched by `export level_api`), globals with `@`, names starting with `_`.
    - unused imports, except those with module parameter values.
    - unreachable statements (`Unreachable`): one per block, at the statement after one that never finishes; nested blocks are checked only up to that statement.
    - library modules (`Module.IsLibrary`, set by the loader) are skipped, unless `warnLibrary` (used by the repository test).
  - `TernCompiler.Compile(..., WarningMode warnings, bool warnLibrary)`: `Report`, `Off`, `AsErrors`. Warnings are `CompileError`s with `IsWarning`, sorted by place (`ErrorList.Sort`) in `CompileResult.Warnings`. An error after the checks (call graph, allocation, layout) carries them in `CompileError.WithWarnings`, sorted with it. `AsErrors` adds each warning to the `ErrorList` as an error and throws, before the call graph. `Compile` is split into the checks and `Build`.
  - *Tools* (`db0e8c6`). `tern` and `tern run`: `file:line:col: warning: ...`, then "2 warnings" / "1 error, 2 warnings" (`Diagnostic.Count`); `--max-errors` counts errors only. `--json`: `"severity": "warning"`, `ok` true with only warnings; `tern run --json` has a `diagnostics` field (warnings). `--no-warnings`, `--warnings-as-errors`; both: "--no-warnings and --warnings-as-errors cannot be given together". `tern bench` compiles with warnings off. Help text updated.
  - *Report* (`8b33229`): "Functions with memory reachable from two roots:", one line per function with a frame (`  f (1 bytes): main, h1, h2`), before the zero-page map; inline functions and functions with no memory are not listed.
  - *Repository*: `RepositoryWarningTests` compiles every example (binaries first, writing their `.prg` and `_api.tern`) and every library module with `warnLibrary`: no warnings. Raster's unused `BAND` removed; speedcode's `cycle_plain` (kept for comparison, main does not call it) is now `_cycle_plain` (the guide's table, spec session's, still says `cycle_plain`; question 117). `SkillTests` compiles the skill's programs with `--warnings-as-errors`; `decl.tern` uses `WIDTH` and `buffer`, `flow.tern` writes `_` for two counters it never read.
  - *Skill* (`3ff5070`): SKILL.md shows `tidy.tern` with all four warnings (a new ```` ```tern warning ```` block kind in `SkillTests`), what each means, `_`, the switches, `run`/`bench`, and the report's list. The unreachable warning points at the start of an assignment (its position was the operator's).
  - Tests: `WarningTests` (29, including the report's list and an allocation error), `WarningToolTests` (9), `RepositoryWarningTests` (2).

Help text and skill updated.

  - Suggestion 1 (`4a9d900`): `pub fn helper()` and `let count` break their names: `Parser.DeclaredName` skips one word before `fn`/`var`/`const`/`data`/`struct` and takes the name after `let`, and the skip after a top-level error stops at such a line (`StartsCurrentDeclaration`). The error for the word itself is unchanged (habit errors would be a spec change).
  - Suggestion 2 (`b0b85cf`): after an unclosed `(`, a line that starts no further right than the failed statement ends the skip, and after an error ends a statement in `ExpectEndOfStatement` (`_statementColumn`, restored when a block ends). Programs without errors parse as before.

- **M23: several errors per compile** (2026-11-12, branch `m23-errors`). Three commits.
  - *Recovery* (`49529bd`). `Syntax/ErrorList.cs`: the errors of a compile in the order found (the same message at the same place once), `ErrorLimitReached` for `--max-errors`, and `FollowOnError`, which stops a statement with no error of its own. `TernCompiler.Compile(..., maxErrors)` throws the first error; `CompileError.All` has every one and `MoreNotReported` says the limit stopped it. Without an `ErrorList`, `Parser.Parse`, `Lexer.Tokenize`, `ModuleLoader.Load` and `Checker.Check` throw the first error as before.
    - Lexer: after an error, the rest of the line is skipped and an `Error` token left; the parser drops the statement holding it without an error of its own.
    - Parser (`Parser.cs`, "Going on after an error"): a failed statement becomes an `ErrorStmt` (with the name of a `var`/`const` it declares), and parsing goes on after the next line break or `;` outside the braces the statement opened (`;` inside `( )`/`[ ]` does not count), or at the `}` that closes the block. A failed top-level declaration is skipped to the next line, outside braces, that starts a declaration; its name (and a failed import's) goes into `ProgramSyntax.BrokenNames`. A `const fn` with an error in its body is dropped whole. "Block is not closed" is reported once (not again for each block around it). After an error, a line that starts a declaration ends the statement before it (an unclosed `(` stops the lexer's line breaks). A line starting with `==` also drops the statement before it, which the checker would report too. `HasSyntaxErrors` silences "The program has no 'fn main()'".
    - Module loader: an import that cannot be loaded is reported, and its name broken.
    - Checker: `Recover` runs each declaration and statement check, reports its error and restores the state (scopes, loop registers, `_dead`, the module and function). `Declaring` also breaks the declaration's name: an `ErrorSymbol` in the file's scope, which `Lookup` turns into a `FollowOnError`. The same for a local, a failed import's module name, module parameters after a failed parameter value, and a name reported as not declared ("'x' is not declared" and "'f' is not a function" for an undeclared call), so each such name is reported once. A struct whose layout failed (`LayoutState` 3) and the regions of a failed memory block add no errors. Functions whose declaration failed are not checked. An `if`/`while` condition is checked on its own, so the branches are checked after an error in it. The call graph (recursion, roots, shared functions), lowering, allocation, code generation and layout run only without errors, and report one error.
  - *Tools* (`f4b3d04`). Text output lists each error, then "3 errors" ("; more errors were not reported (--max-errors n)" when the limit stopped it). `--json` keeps its format, one entry per error. `--max-errors n` for `tern`, `tern run` and `tern bench`. `tern bench` lists every error of a variant (and their number when several); its JSON keeps `error` (the first) and adds `errors`. The skill says one compile lists every error, and its `broken.tern` has two.
  - *Fix sentences* (`049e978`). `FixSentenceTests` reads every message in `src/Tern.Compiler` (`new CompileError(pos, ...)`, the interpreter's `Error(pos, ...)`, each `?:` branch on its own) and fails for one without a suggestion unless it is in `NoFix` with the reason. 317 had none: about 290 got a fix sentence; `Define`, `Name`, `Separate` and `Start` are new fix verbs; some fixes were reworded to start a sentence. `NoFix`: another error's text passed on with a note, the program's assert message, the parser's "Expected X, found Y" and "Expected an expression", "Expected T, found a number".
  - Tests: `SeveralErrorsTests` (26: the roadmap's cases, syntax recovery, lexer errors, broken names of each kind, the CLI, `run` and `bench`), `FixSentenceTests` (2). Every existing error test gets its error first; tests quoting the old wording were updated.

- **M22: `const fn` and code generators** (2026-11-11, spec 4.1, 12.3; decisions 2026-11-03 and 2026-11-10). One commit per step.
  - *Question 92* (`870ab73`, spec 12.3): `Checker.MatchInterface` records each volatile interface variable with the binary's own `data` of the same name (`_binaryData`); a use of it in code, asm or `addr()` (`UseGlobal`) is "'level_api.header' is this binary's data 'header'. Use 'header'". Another fixed variable over the data keeps the general overlap error. `PluginTests`.
  - *Part 1* (`6cabf70`): a tree-walking interpreter in `CompileTime/` (`Interpreter`, `Values`: `CtInt`, `CtReal`, `CtBool`, `CtArray`, `CtLabel`; `Types.Resolve`). It talks to the checker through `ICompileTimeHost` (names read in the const fn's module with `InModule`, data tables decoded once). Lexer: `TokenKind.Real` (`1.5`, `2e3`, `25e-1`; `0..25` stays a range). Parser: `const fn` (in `ProgramSyntax.ConstFns`, not `Functions`, so nothing compiles them), `[expr for i in range]` (`ListForExpr`), `[value; count]` (`ArrayRepeatExpr`), `emit { }` (`EmitStmt`, contextual) and a generator call line in asm (`AsmGeneratorCall`). Checker (`Checker.CompileTime.cs`): const fns are declared before everything else, with a body check (`ConstFnBodyCheck`: runtime types, `@`, asm, names already declared, return and emit rules). In a constant, a data or global initial value or a local `const`, an expression with a compile-time part (`NeedsInterpreter`: a real, a list, `PI`/`TAU`, a const fn or a built-in) is computed whole by the interpreter (`EvaluateConstant`); in runtime code it is an error (`InRuntime`). A compile-time array fills a table (`ArrayBytes`, each element checked) with a checked length or `[T; _]`. Step limit 10,000,000 per top-level evaluation (about 1 s to reach); an error inside a const fn gets "(called from main.tern:5)" after its first sentence. `Check` is a fix verb (the spec's step-limit message). `ConstFnTests` (done when: a sine table and a row table from a module's const fns against C# `Math`; every math function against C#; every error).
  - *Part 2* (`6f8e579`): `Checker.ExpandGenerators` replaces each generator call line with what `Interpreter.Generate` emits. Operands: compile-time parts become numbers; variables, data and functions stay names, bound in the generator's module (`NameExpr.Bound`); an emit's `.labels` are renamed per emit (`.skip#3`); `label()` values are placed with `name:` and used as operands (`.label#N`), checked placed once and never unplaced. Generated lines carry `CalledFrom`: an error in one (checker or assembler, e.g. a branch out of range) names the emit line and the call, and the listing shows the call line, then each instruction under its emit line with "(generated by line N)" (`SourceLine.Via`). `GeneratorTests` (done when: an unrolled screen copy skipping rows 3, 12 and 24 runs in the emulator, and the listing shows each instruction's source).
  - *Skill* (`374d8ca`): `language.md` section 13 (const fn, lists, generators), with an example `SkillTests` runs; the library is section 14.

Rename your file, e.g. to my_io.tern, and import it by its new name". Not for a main file of that name (never imported), nor for library modules importing each other (`InLibrary`). The old test of the library hiding a program file now checks the two folders without a clash. `ModuleTests` (io in `lib/c64`, random in `lib/`, from another program module, the `--json` suggestion, library modules in a platform folder).
  - *`export level_api`* (`c4440ee`): a name on the export line that is a module the main file imports. `Checker.MatchInterface` matches each routine of the module (by its name in the module's scope) to a function of the main file with the same parameter types and registers and the same return (`Signature.Matches`; names may differ); `setup` must be a routine at the start of the first program area with no parameters or return; routines must not overlap in the table; each non-`data` fixed-address variable of the module needs `data` of the same name, type and address. The table is laid out in address order, zeros in gaps (`TernCompiler`). After code generation, a function (or `setup`'s code) that changes a register its routine `keeps` is an error. `name_api.tern` is still written. `PluginTests` (placement, calls through the interface's addresses, a gap, every error).
  - *`export f, g @ addr` in a program with `main`* (`f0f7170`): `ProgramSyntax.ExportTable`; a fixed section "export table" with one `jmp` per function, in a program or reserved area (`CheckExportTable`). The entries are address-taken (`AddressTaken`, `main.UsedAddresses`), so they are compiled and their frames overlap no other. `BinaryInterface.GenerateTable` writes `name_api.tern` with the entries and their `keeps` only. The report has "Export table at $0900:". `CompileResult.Entries` lists the table. `PluginTests`.

Checked in VICE from a disk image (commands in `game.tern`'s header): "FOREST CAVE 80" printed, border grey. `PluginTests.The_example_game_loads_two_levels_built_separately_into_one_slot_and_they_call_its_table` traps SETNAM/SETLFS/LOAD/CHROUT to load the level files in turn.

A memory block that needs itself through such a constant gets its own error. `ConstantOrderTests.Len_of_a_global_in_a_region_in_a_constant`.

- **M20 follow-up 3** (2026-11-09, decisions "Galaxy Stars port, remaining gaps"). One commit per item.
  - *Evaluation order* (`ea9cf42`, spec 5, gap 12): `CodeGen.InSourceOrder` computes the first of two parts that both call a function or read volatile memory (`HasEffect`) into a temporary first: the two sides of a binary operator (`GenBinaryA`, `Gen16`, `GenCompare`, `Assign`), an index before the assigned value. `GenCall` computes an argument with an effect early when a later argument has one too. A commutative 16-bit operator with a simple left operand computes the right side straight into the destination, so `fib16`'s `mul(...) + mul(...)` (two calls in one statement, which the roadmap thought no benchmark had) costs the same. `dst[i] = src[i]` with a volatile `i` reads `i` for the destination first, into a temporary (4 bytes more); the volatile tests count every read. `EvaluationOrderTests` (31 of its 47 cases fail without the change).
  - *Constants on demand* (`5025531`, spec 2.2, 4, gaps 11 and 17): `Checker.Lookup` declares a top-level constant that is not declared yet when a name needs it (`Demand`, in its own module through `InModule`, which saves and restores the checker's state); while a constant or a global's type is worked out, a global is declared the same way, so `len(t)` works in a constant. Struct names and module parameters of all files are declared before any constant; a struct is laid out in its own module. A cycle is "'X' depends on itself: a.X -> b.Y -> a.X. Give one of them a value that does not use the others" (`StartDeclaring`, also through a global's length). The copy of the main file for parameter values declares no globals. The "import each other" error remains for what is not a constant, such as `addr(a.fa)` in a global of b. `ConstantOrderTests`; `ModuleTests`' cycle test.
  - *`var` lines in a plain `asm fn`* (`9bcdac7`, spec 9, gap 15): the locals are in its frame (`AsmFnLocalsTests`: frame sharing, the same name in two functions, a `(p),y` pointer in zero page, the errors). The messages say "an asm fn" and give a fix.
  - *`[u8; _]` from a string* (`770bb62`, spec 4, gap 24), encoding prefix and `\0` included; the `_` error says "a list or string initial value". `DataTests`.
  - *`step`* (`45a11c6`, spec 13, gap 18): no longer in the lexer's keywords; `ParseFor` takes the name `step` after the range. The keyword-name example is `fn match()` (`ErrorTests`, `errors.md`). `StepNameTests`.
  - *A line starting with a binary operator* (`f62162e`, spec 2, gap 19): `Parser.LeadingOperator` ("A line cannot start with '&&': ... Move '&&' to the end of the line before, ..."), in a statement, a header and at the top level; not for `+` / `-` (values) nor `*` (its C-habit error). `ErrorTests`.
  - *Importing the main file* (`066ce06`, spec 2.2, gap 23): `ModuleLoader` reports it at the import, before looking for the file: "'main' is the program's main file, which cannot be imported. Move what demo needs from it into a module that both import". `ModuleTests`.
  - *`c64.cia_irqs_off()`* (`eb177f3`, spec 10.1): a plain `asm fn` (`lda #$7F`, both `sta icr`, both `lda icr`). `examples/kernal_irq.tern` and `language.md` use it. Checked in VICE from a disk image: blue border above line 150, red below, "00, OK,00,00" printed. `LibraryTests`.
  - *Spec 11 rows* (`5b8239b`, gaps 13 and 14): `p = q + e` with `e` an unsigned byte that needs code and `q` used in place (a variable or an element) is `e / clc / adc q / sta p / lda q+1 / adc #0 / sta p+1` (`CodeGen.ByteAddend`); the port's `tile_row_addr[row] + (col << 1)` is 18 bytes before the `rts`. A signed `s < 0` / `s >= 0` is `lda s` (`s+1` for an `i16`) and `bmi` / `bpl` (`GenCompare`). `SignAndByteAddTests`. No benchmark and no skill output moved.
  - *Skill* (`a9009d6`): `language.md` has the evaluation-order rule and its exception near the operators, line continuation in section 1, and `var` lines in `asm fn` in section 10.
  - *Done when* (`0ee968a`): `GalaxyStarsGapTests.The_workarounds_for_gaps_11_to_24_are_written_plainly` (video, stars and explode as an import ring in the port's shape; the specks against a C# model of the port's generator).

- **Questions 86 and 87, after the review** (2026-11-09, decisions 2026-11-09, roadmap M20 follow-up 2 "after the review").
  - 87: a number literal may be up to 2^63 - 1 (spec 2.1). `Lexer` checks `value > (long.MaxValue - digit) / radix` before each digit, so the accumulation never overflows; the error is "Number is too large. Use a value up to $7FFFFFFFFFFFFFFF (9223372036854775807)". `ConstantRangeTests` writes `MAX` as `9223372036854775807` and `MIN` as `-9223372036854775807 - 1`, and tests the largest decimal, hex (with and without `_`) and binary literals, one above each, larger ones, `-9223372036854775808` (an error: the literal has no sign), and the lowest value.
  - 86: struct literal fields stay separated by `,` or a newline, as built. `StructLiteralTests.Reports_errors` checks that a newline between list elements (`[` / `1` / `2` / `]`) is still the missing-`,` error.

  - *String-literal error* (`0dcedc3`, spec 2.1): "A string can only be the value of data or a global array. Declare it as data, e.g. 'data HI = "HI"', and pass addr(HI), len(HI)"; `tern --json` gives the second sentence as the suggestion. `DiagnosticTests`.

Use smaller values", at the step's operator. A constant shift count is 0 to 63 (was 31). `MIN % -1` is 0. Assembly operands (`FoldAsm`) use the same arithmetic. `ConstantRangeTests` (edges, the rounding of `/`, `%`, `>>` on negatives, every overflow, the roadmap's example).

No code: `BlockStmt.Code` is the statements without constants, used by `GenBlock` (so a const is not "the statement before"), the F9 while shapes and `AlwaysReturns`. `LocalConstTests`; `language.md`.

The skill's ring example: 3905 -> 3217 cycles.
  - *Spec 11 rows as built* (`5cf2f10`): `CompareShapeTests`, a listing test with results for the signed and 16-bit compares, `&&`, `||`, a compare stored in a `bool` and `i8 >> 1`. No compiler change.
  - *Struct literals* (`94652af`, spec 3.3, 8.5): `StructLiteralExpr` in the parser (not in `if` / `while` / `for` / `match` headers: `ParseHeaderExpr`); list literals skip line breaks and allow a trailing `,`. `Checker.StructBytes` / `FieldBytes` / `ScalarInto` build a record in its memory layout; an address in a 16-bit array field (split inside the record) is two half fills (`AddressBytes(..., highAt)`). `TernCompiler.ByteLayout` / `LayoutIndex`: a non-linear array of structs is one table per record byte. `data` may be a struct. A literal in code or as a local's initial value: "A struct literal is only the initial value of a global or of data ... Assign the fields one by one, e.g. 'p.x = 1'". `StructLiteralTests` (every layout against C#, addresses, array fields, the 8.5 example in an overlay, each error); `GalaxyStarsPagesTests` is the "done when" (the `how_row` / `how_col` / `how_text` group). Four `StructTests` / `DataTests` cases now expect the new messages. `language.md` section 5 has a checked example.

- **Questions 84 and 85** (2026-11-09).
  - 84: `.lo` / `.hi` on `addr(...) ± constant` in code. The checker wraps such a target (a pointer `BinaryExpr` that `Placed` accepts) in a `u16` cast before the field lookup, so a struct field named `hi` does not interfere; a runtime offset keeps the pointer error. `CodeGen.AddressBytes` gives the two bytes of `addr(x)` plus or minus constants through two-byte casts (used by `Simple2`), so `(addr(map) + 299).hi` is `lda #(map+299).hi`.
  - 85: `Describe` writes a call with its arguments, so `n + addr(map)` suggests "Write 'addr(map) + n'".
  - `AddressConversionTests` (4 new); `language.md` gives the offset form and the pointer-first rule.

Finding 2 (must fix): `ConstSymbol.IsBitPattern` keeps `const N = ~$0F` a bit pattern; a pattern wider than the type has its own error. Suggestion 3 is moot after question 81; 4 is question 83 (below); 5: "this value is i8"; 6: a zero-page startup test.
  - Question 79: `Unify` picks the operation's type with the constant's written type as a variable's (`TypedOperationType`), then checks the constant at that width (`w & ~M` is `$FFF0`, `w + BOX * 30` adds 390). A compound assignment with a typed constant gets the same type check (`x += BIG` with `BIG: u16` is an error).

  - Tests: `ConstantWidthTests`, `AddressConversionTests`, updated expectations elsewhere. The Galaxy Stars port and all examples compile.

- **M20 follow-up** (2026-11-09). One commit per item.
  - *Tools* (`cdd76c0`): `tern run --set $DC00=$7B` (and `tern bench --set`; decimal or `$`, may be repeated) writes a byte after the program is loaded, before it starts (`RunOptions.Sets`, `MemorySet`). Addresses only (question 82). `tern bench` runs each variant with the index check and prints its errors (a snippet's on its own lines); a variant with any fails: exit code 1, `ok: false` and `indexErrors` in `--json`. `RunToolTests`, `BenchToolTests`; `SKILL.md`.
  - *`v += 2` / `v -= 2` on a variable held in X or Y* (`d8216e8`, spec 11): `inx / inx` (`dex`, `iny`, `dey`) in `GenAssign`; `HoldInRegister` counts `v += 2` as a use the register saves, like `v += 1`. `HeldVariableTests`.
  - *`[T; _]`* (`59b49e3`, spec 4): `ArrayTypeSyntax.Length` is null for `_`; `DeclareGlobal` passes the list's length (`_listLength`) to `ResolveType`, which checks it as a written length (long byte data too). Anywhere else (no list, a string, a local, a parameter, a field) "The length '_' is taken from a list initial value ... Write the length here". The "need a written type" errors suggest `[u8; _]`. `DataTests`.
  - *`addr()` in initial values* (`87a9b5d`, spec 4): `AddressFill` (function, or variable with a field's offset, plus an offset, word or `.lo` / `.hi`) replaces `FunctionAddresses` (now derived). `Checker.AddressBytes` / `Placed` accept `addr(x)`, `± constant` (either side of `+`), `as` to a two-byte type, and `.lo` / `.hi` into a `u8` (question 83); anything else is an error saying what is allowed. Initial values with `addr()` are worked out after the file's last global, so they may name later globals or themselves. An overlay cannot name a sibling's function or variable. A kept global keeps the globals (and their functions) it names (`GlobalsNamedBy`). Backend: `AddressByte` (one byte of a label's or variable's address plus an offset, listed as `lo: (name+off)`), and the zero-page startup loads `#ImmVarByte` / `#ImmLabelByte`. `AddressInitTests`, `OverlayTests`; three tests of the old error are gone.
  - *Exact constant expressions* (`cd81385`, spec 3.1, 3.4): an integer constant, typed or not, is an `IntLiteral` in expressions, computed exactly; a typed one carries `Expr.ConstType` / `ConstTypeFrom` (an untyped constant computed from typed ones inherits it, question 81). That type applies when mixed with a runtime value (`Unify`), with no context (`DefaultType`), and to a `for` bound; not to `as`, `.lo`, `.hi` (`LiteralType`), nor to an assignment or argument, where it fits like a literal (question 80). `~` of a constant is exact and a bit pattern (`IsBitPattern`, kept through `& | ^` and shifts), which takes the width of an unsigned type it is used as. A literal that does not fit next to a runtime operand names the operand (`CoerceNamed`, `Describe`): "300 does not fit in u8 (0 to 255), the type of 'x', which it is combined with. Convert 'x' with 'as u16'". A constant expression that wrapped is now an error. `ConstantExpressionTests`; `language.md`, `errors.md`.
  - *`c64.sprite_xy` and `c64.set_kernal_irq`* (`d6c58f7`, spec 10.1): `sprite_xy: [u8; 16] @ $D000`; `set_kernal_irq` points `$0314` at a private stub (`kernal_entry`: `pla / tay / pla / tax / pla / jmp (kernal_handler)`). `examples/kernal_irq.tern` (raster split, drive status through `io`), checked in VICE from a disk image (commands in its header): blue above line 150, red below, "00, OK,00,00" printed. `C64LibraryTests` run a handler through the KERNAL's own IRQ entry bytes at `$FF48` (`KernalIrqEntry`). `language.md`: when a handler gets `cld`, `set_kernal_irq` and its limits.
  - *Done when* (`7ca5e73`): `GalaxyStarsGapTests` runs gaps 1, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8, 9 and 10 in their plain form, from the port's code. The port (`galaxy-star/tern/src/main.tern`, `check_player.tern`) still compiles unchanged.

The checker tags the shift it builds (`BinaryExpr.ScalesIndexOf`); its first `asl` carries `Instr.Check` (`IndexCheck.InA`: a check on A, not part of the instruction's identity), which `IndexChecker` tests before the shift. Code unchanged. `IndexCheckTests` has the review's two programs and a register counter from 125.
