- **M16, locals of inline functions in each caller's frame** (2026-11-07).
  - `CodeGen.MoveInlineFrame`: after the copy of an inline function (arguments and body) is generated, every operand that is a variable of the inline function (parameter, local, loop counter, temporary; `Owner == f`) is replaced by a copy owned by the function being generated (`_frameOwner`), added to its `FrameVariables`. One set per (caller, inline function): all copies in one caller share it (as before, and as `_inlineTemps` already did), two callers have two sets. The copy is named `caller.function.name` (`main.math.min8.tmp`) and carries the original's flags (`Dereferenced`, `AsmZeroPage`, `RequireZeroPage`, `AddressTaken`, ...); `VariableSymbol.InlineOf` names the inline function. Weights are copied at the end of `GenerateFunctions`, which also empties each inline function's `FrameVariables`, so it has no frame and `CheckSharedFunctions` no longer rejects an inline function with locals reached from two roots.
  - What stays an error: a function an inline function calls is still one function (`g` with memory called through `f` from main and a handler); a function reached from two roots that calls an inline function with locals ("the variable 't' of the inline function 'f', which is copied into it").
  - `var` lines at the start of an `inline asm fn` body (`AsmStmt.Locals`, `Parser.ParseAsmBody(isFn)`, `Checker.CheckLocal(inAsmFn)`): scalars, arrays or structs, no initial value (the assembly sets it), named in the instructions like any local. Errors: after the first instruction, with an initial value, in a plain `asm fn`, in an `asm { }` block statement.
  - Library: `math.min8`, `max8`, `clamp8` and the signed ones use a local (`tmp`; `ta`/`tb`; `tx`/`tlo`/`thi`) instead of the private `n0`-`n2`, which are gone. `math.min8` in main and in a handler compiles and runs (`SignedMathTests.Min8_from_main_and_a_handler_compiles_and_runs`); the old test that expected the private-variable error is replaced by it.
  - The optimizer now treats an inline function's variables as the caller's own (`Owner` is the caller), so a write to them no longer counts as a write to a callee's frame. No benchmark changed.
  - Tests (`InlineLocalsTests`): one inline function with locals called from main and from a handler under `irqEvery`, results compared with C# for both (a shared set would be corrupted by an interrupt between statements); the symbol table shows `main.f.t` and `h.f.t` at different addresses; copies in one caller share one set; nested inline functions with a local array; loop counters and a dereferenced pointer parameter (zero page) per caller; a function and main both calling the inline function; an `inline asm fn` with a `var` line from main and a handler; the two errors above.
  - Not done: a `for` loop counter that an inline function put in a register keeps its register in every copy (`ChooseCounterRegister` decides once per function, from the first copy's context; unchanged from before M16).

- **M15, indexing small linear struct arrays** (2026-11-07).
  - `linear var list: [T; n]` with a variable index: the checker lowers `list[i].f` to a view of the array at the field's offset, indexed by `i << k` for struct size 1, 2, 4, 8 (`Checker.CheckRecordIndex`, new `PlaceKind.Records`). The code generator is unchanged for this: `lda i / asl / asl / tax / lda list+off,x`. Size 1 is no shift: `ldx i / lda list,x`. Linear struct arrays of one-byte structs are now allowed (they were an error "elements are one byte").
  - `indexed var list: [T; n]` (a word only before `var`, also after `noinit`, `volatile`, `private`; also for locals): same layout as `linear`; a variable index goes through a hidden read-only table `<name>.offsets` of `i * sizeof(T)`, created at the first variable index and kept only if used (`VariableSymbol.OffsetTable`, `IsOffsetTable`). `CodeGen.LoadIndex` loads it straight into Y: `ldx i / ldy list.offsets,x / lda list+off,y`; with a `using X` loop variable it is `ldy list.offsets,x`. The listing shows the table after main's code; the report has a line `Offset table list.offsets: 20 bytes, in the program`.
  - Constant index into a linear or indexed array: a fixed address (`list+295`), any struct size and any array size.
  - Padding fields `_`: several allowed, may have `@`, take space, error on read or write.
  - Errors (all tested in `RecordIndexTests.Reports_errors`): record size not 1, 2, 4, 8 on a variable index (names the size, suggests `_` padding and `indexed`, with the table size); variable index into a linear array over 256 bytes (suggests a pointer); `indexed` on a non-struct array, on one over 256 bytes, on `data`, with `linear` in either order; `addr()` of a field with a variable index; variable index into an array field of such a record.
  - Code generator, beyond the milestone: `arr[i] = v` and `arr[i] op= v` where the index needs code (a shift, the offset table, `a[j + 1]`) now load the index first and the value after, instead of saving A in a temporary or computing the index twice. Without it the record stores cost 4 to 6 extra instructions. It also shortened `bubble` (116 -> 112 bytes, 241171 -> 227959 cycles).
  - Removed the `StructTests` case that expected "'ps' is linear, so it cannot be indexed" for a constant index; constant indexes work now.
  - Not done: the `linear` 16-bit element arrays and pointers are unchanged; `data` cannot hold structs, so `indexed data` is an error.

- **M14, C64 device I/O library** (2026-11-07).
  - `src/lib/c64/io.tern` (module `io`). Raw KERNAL routines at their addresses (spec 6.6, direct `jsr`): `setlfs`, `setnam`, `kernal_open`, `close`, `chkin`, `chkout`, `clrchn`, `chrin`, `chrout`, `readst`, `kernal_load` (A fixed to 0). `OPEN` is `kernal_open` because `open` is the wrapper below. `LOAD`/`SAVE` raw forms: only `kernal_load` is public; `SAVE` is inside `save` (it needs a zero-page pointer).
  - Whole files: `load(name, n, device, address: ptr u8) -> bool` (secondary address 0: to the given address); `save(name, n, device, first, last: ptr u8) -> bool` (`asm fn`; `last` is included, the end passed to SAVE is `last + 1`, so `last` must be below `$FFFF`, a KERNAL limit, documented; the start goes through a private `@ zp` pointer; secondary address 1). Both return the KERNAL's carry.
  - Sequential files: `open(lfn, device, channel, name, n) -> bool`, `read(lfn, dst, max) -> u8` (stops at end of file, which counts the last byte, at a timeout bit (a missing file: status $42 at the first read), which counts nothing, or at `max`), `write(lfn, src, n) -> bool` (true on carry or any status bit), `close`, `status() -> u8` (inline `readst`). Counts are `u8`, like `c64.print`; larger blocks are several calls. Constants `ST_EOF`, `ST_TIMEOUT`, `ST_NO_DEVICE`.
  - Command channel (review finding 1: closing channel 15 closes every file on a real drive, so it is opened once and closed last): `command_open(device) -> bool`, `command_close()`, `command(cmd, n) -> bool` (sends the text and a CR through the open channel), `drive_status(dst, max) -> u8` (reads the message from the open channel, without the CR, and leaves it open; 0 if the channel is not open or the drive does not answer), `command_once(device, cmd, n) -> bool` (open, send, close; only when no file is open on the drive; closes only if the open succeeded).
  - `c64.load` is now plain Tern on `io.setnam`/`setlfs`/`kernal_load_own` (LOAD with no address parameter): same signature, same call sequence (`SETNAM`, `SETLFS` 1,device,1, `LOAD`).
  - Tests: `IoTests` (38), KERNAL and a disk drive as a C# class (`FakeKernal`) behind `Run.Program` traps: arguments and call order of every function, carry results, end of file, and the real-1541 behavior from the review (closing channel 15 closes all files, a missing file gives no error at open and status $42 at the first read, a send clears the status), a save followed by a load, every drive-status message case, and `examples/highscore.tern`.
  - Example: `examples/highscore.tern` (commands for the disk image in its header).


- **M13 follow-up** (2026-11-07; settled 2026-11-03/04).
  - *`volatile` over an overlay (spec 8.5).* A `volatile` fixed-address variable may overlap an overlay's file; without it the error suggests `volatile` or the overlay's own name. Overlap with the resident program is still an error. A write to a fixed variable that lies in an overlay area (`VariableSymbol.InOverlayArea`, set in the checker) makes the optimizer forget every variable (`Optimizer.ForgetWrite`), like a write through a pointer, whatever other volatile ranges it is in. Overlap is now checked against each section of the overlay, not its whole span.
  - *Fixed data in an overlay module (question 58).* `SectionStart` of such data carries the module; the assembler lays the module's other sections around it (first fit in the area, `Assembler`), gaps zero-filled, and the file starts at the area's start. Fixed data of two overlays may share an address. Errors (`CheckFixedData`): outside the module's own area; a resident module's or the main file's fixed data in an overlay area; overlap with the module's other fixed data (assembler) or fixed variables (`CheckFixedAddresses`). Unused data is dropped, so the checks apply only to data that is used.
  - *Sibling uses (question 56).* `addr()` of a function of another overlay in the same area is an error (`TakeFunctionAddress`); using fixed data of a sibling too. Resident code and overlays in other areas may still take these addresses.
  - *Zero page (question 55).* A fixed zero-page variable (`var p: ptr u8 @ $FB`) already worked in an overlay (not in the file, shared with the main program); tested. The `@ zp` error suggests it.
  - *Long byte buffers (spec 3.2).* `var x: [u8; n]` / `[i8; n]` with `n` above 256 for a global var (`_allowLongBytes` in `Checker`); other element types, locals, struct fields and a var with an initial value are errors; indexing was already an error that suggests `addr(x)`. `LongBufferTests`.
  - *`const` before the `memory` block (spec 8.4).* `Parser`: after the imports, `const` lines may precede the block; the other-declaration-before-the-block error keeps its test with the new message.
  - *Tests:* 26 new in `OverlayTests`, `MemoryTests` and `LongBufferTests`. The optimizer test (`hdr += 1` leaves A alone, then `ov.x` must be loaded again) checks the listing and the result, with and without a `volatile` range over the area.

- **M13, overlays** (2026-11-03).
  - **Follow-ups** (roadmap, settled 2026-11-02/03):
    - *Handler-side helpers (48).* `Checker.HandlerSideFunctions`: a function of a module that only the module's own `interrupt fn`s call, directly or through other such functions, may use the module's private variables; one that is also reached from main-side code is still the error (even if the module's handler calls it too). `sprites.put` is a plain `fn`. Tests in `InterruptTests`.
    - *Constant conditions before reachability (49).* The checker marks the branch of a constant `if`/`while` that is never taken as dead (`_dead`): it is still checked, but calls, globals, `addr()` and handlers named in it are not recorded, and `GenIf` emits only the branch taken. `import sprites(COUNT = 24)` without `FLICKER` went from 537 to 391 bytes of code and data and from 648 to 600 bytes of variables. `ConstantConditionTests`.
    - *A region named like a symbol (51).* An error when the name is a constant, variable, function or imported module of the main file (`MemoryTests.Reports_errors`).
    - *`{` on the next line.* `Parser.SkipBreaksBeforeBrace` for `fn`, `interrupt fn`, `asm fn`, `if`, `else`, `while`, `for`, `match`, `struct`, `asm` and `memory`; `BraceTests` has one program with every construct, and a brace after a line break anywhere else is still an error.
    - `math.min8` etc. already had their own work bytes (M10 follow-up, `SignedMathTests`).
  - **Syntax and layout.** `overlay name` lines in the memory block (`MemoryLayout.Overlays`; taken out of the program areas and free zero page like regions; a name used twice, or as a region too, is an error). `import name @ area` after the optional parameters (`ImportDecl.Area`); only in the main file; the area must be an overlay area (a region, or an unknown name, gets its own message). `Module.OverlayArea`, and `Module` / `OverlayArea` on function and global symbols.
  - **Code and memory.** Each overlay module's functions, `data` and global variables are sections tagged with the module (`SectionStart.Overlay`). The assembler lays each module out from the start of its area, so overlays in one area overlap; the whole program is still one assembly, so labels resolve across resident code and overlays. Variables are bytes of the file after the code: initial value, or zeros (`noinit` makes no difference). The startup code, `bss` and the zero-page allocator do not see them. Frames (locals, parameters) of overlay functions are allocated as for any function, in resident RAM and zero page.
  - **Output.** `CompileResult.Overlays` (`OverlayFile`: name, area, load address, image, `Prg`); the CLI writes `<module>.prg` next to the output file; the report lists each area and the files in it with their size. A module placed in an area but with nothing reachable gets no file.
  - **Errors.** A call between two modules of the same area (also in assembly: `jsr`/`jmp`), at the call; use of another overlay's global variable in the same area (they share memory), at the name; an overlay that does not fit its area ("needs N bytes, area has M"); an area that is not declared or is a region; `@` in a module; a zero-page-forced global (`@ zp`, a dereferenced pointer) in an overlay; an inline function of another overlay in the same area that uses that overlay's variables or calls its functions (`CheckInlineCallsBetweenOverlays`, at the call); a fixed-address variable overlapping an overlay's file. The "does not fit" error is reported at the `import` line. A call from resident code into an overlay is a plain `jsr`.
  - **Library.** `c64.load(name: ptr u8, n: u8, device: u8) -> bool` is an `asm fn`: SETNAM, SETLFS (logical file 1, secondary address 1 = load to the file's own address), LOAD; returns the carry (true = failed).
  - **Tests.** `OverlayTests` (23): two overlays loaded in turn with initial values restored on reload, the file contents, shared addresses, resident modules called from overlays, overlays in different areas calling each other, an unused overlay, every error above, `c64.load` against stand-ins for the three KERNAL routines (arguments and carry), and `examples/levels.tern`. `Run.Program` has a new `traps` argument: stand-ins for ROM routines, run when the CPU reaches the address, then returning as `rts`.
  - **Example.** `examples/levels.tern` with `level1.tern` and `level2.tern`; the commands for the disk image and VICE are in its header comment.

- **M12, memory layout and binaries without `main`** (2026-11-03).
  - **Syntax.** `memory { first..=last kind [name] }` right after the imports (`program`, `zeropage`, `reserved`, `region name`, `volatile`; `overlay name` parses and gives "'overlay' is not supported yet"). Kinds are words only inside the block (`volatile` is a keyword anyway, so the parser accepts both). `export a, b` is a top-level declaration; `export` is reserved. `@ name` after a `var`/`data` is a region when the name is one of the block's regions and nothing else is declared with that name; otherwise an address expression. `data x: T @ addr = ...` and `@ region` work; `@ zp` on data is an error.
  - **`MemoryLayout`** (`Semantics/MemoryLayout.cs`): evaluated by the checker once the main file's constants are declared, so ranges can use constants from imports and constants declared below the block. `program`/`zeropage` lines replace the platform's areas; `reserved`, `region` and `overlay` ranges are taken out of the platform's program areas and free zero page when the block does not replace them. Errors: backwards or out-of-memory range, `zeropage` above `$FF`, overlapping ranges (except `volatile`), a region declared twice, no program area left, a `main` program whose first area does not start at `$0801`, a block in a module. `volatile` ranges make fixed-address variables in them volatile (applied after all globals are declared).
  - **Placement** (`Backend/Assembler.cs`): the code generator emits sections (`SectionStart`: the startup code, each function, each initialized global, each fixed data item) and one `Reserve` for the variables. The assembler fills the program areas first fit in order, and a section never crosses an area boundary; data at a fixed address punches a hole the code goes around. The variables go in the first area with room after its code (`RamPlan` offsets from the block's start, as before). A section that does not fit gives an error listing each area with its use and what is in it. `Assembler.Assemble` returns segments; `TernCompiler` writes the file from the lowest to the highest segment, gaps zero-filled; the report says so and lists the areas and regions. Added to `CompileResult`: `Sections`, `Areas`, `Entries`, `ApiModule`, `ApiModuleName`.
  - **Regions** (`Allocator`): variables and data with `@ region` are placed in declaration order at the region's start: zeroed variables first (cleared at startup by one block per region), then `noinit` ones, then data (part of the program file, at that address). A full region is an error. A `var @ region` with an initial value is an error (only data can be initialized there). `@ region` works in the main file only (a module is checked before the block exists).
  - **Zero page** is a list of ranges (flattened in address order). A variable must lie in consecutive bytes: one that would straddle a gap is an error naming the ranges.
  - **Fixed addresses**: data with `@ address` must lie in a program or reserved area; a fixed `var` still may not overlap what the compiler placed (now computed per area), and may not overlap fixed data. The existing messages are unchanged.
  - **Binaries** (`export`): the checker validates the exports (plain functions of the main file, register parameters only, no `setup`, no duplicates, no interrupt/inline/fixed-address functions, no `main` next to them) and gives them one synthetic root (`(entries)`), so their frames and private variables share memory. `TernCompiler` writes the jump table (`jmp setup`, then the exports in order), then `setup` (the startup code, `rts`), then the functions; no BASIC line; `Entry` is the table. `Backend/RegisterUse.cs` works out which of A, X, Y a function changes, through everything it calls (a `jsr`/`jmp` it cannot name counts as changing all three, a routine at a fixed address as changing what it does not keep); `Backend/BinaryInterface.cs` writes `name_api.tern`: `CODE_FIRST/LAST` (the file), `VARS_*` (variables outside the file), `<REGION>_*` (regions outside the file), `ZP_*` (zero page the binary uses), then `fn setup() @ $C000 keeps ...` and one routine per export with `keeps` = not changed and not a return register. A pointer to a struct is written as `u16`. The `tern` tool writes the api file next to the `.prg`; `examples/player_api.tern` is git-ignored.
  - **Tests**: `MemoryTests` (placement in each kind of area, functions whole, the hole around data, a zero-filled gap, regions cleared page by page, several zero-page ranges, volatile ranges, constants from imports, every new error), `BinaryTests` (jump table and no BASIC line, entries called one by one after `setup` in the emulator, order of the export line, shared frames, calls between exports, the `keeps` text and a check that every kept register comes back unchanged, region constants, a Tern host importing the api module with its memory block and calling the binary also from an interrupt handler, the two examples, every new error).
  - **Review fixes** (`docs/reviews/m12.md`, first review). 1: the first section (BASIC line and startup, or the jump table) is laid at the first area's start before anything else; fixed data overlapping it is an error. 2: a region inside a `volatile` range or a platform volatile area makes its variables volatile (decided at the declaration, from the whole region, since addresses come later). 3: `program` lines are taken out of the platform's free zero page. 4: a fixed variable overlapping region variables is an error. 5: the api module has one `ZP`, `ZP2`, ... pair per zero-page range with used bytes. 6: the api module also lists fixed-address variables outside volatile areas (`FIXED*`) and the binary's `reserved` lines (`RESERVED*`), each byte once. R1 (re-review): the startup code or jump table not fitting in the first area is an error. 7: the first-area error says to move the `reserved` range when there is no `program` line; region clear labels are `clear_C000_0`; a comment on `HaltAddress`. "No program area is left" now only arises when reserved ranges cover everything.
  - **Examples**: build `player.tern` first (writes `player.prg` and `player_api.tern`), then `player_host.tern`, which puts `player.prg` into its own file with `data player @ player_api.CODE_FIRST - 2 = include_bytes("player.prg")` (the file's two-byte load address lands below the code) and reserves the player's memory. The 47 KB file loads in VICE with `-limitcycles 400000000` (about 30 seconds in warp); `sprite_data.prg` needs about 60 million. A `.prg` autostarted from disk loads slowly: a screenshot taken too early shows LOADING.
- **M11, C64 graphics library** (2026-11-02).
  - **M10 follow-up**: `math.min8`, `max8`, `clamp8` and the signed ones use private work bytes `n0`-`n2` instead of `w0`-`w2`. Tests (`SignedMathTests`): `mul8` in main with `min8`/`maxs8` in a handler under `irqEvery` gives correct results; `min8` from both main and a handler is still the private-variable error. `BenchmarkTests` unchanged.
  - **Test tool `FakeVic`** (`tests/.../FakeVic.cs`, used through `SpriteTests.Rig`): 63 cycles per line, 312 lines, the raster compare interrupt (`$D012`, `$D011` bit 7, `$D019`, `$D01A`), and sprite display (a sprite starts on the line its y register holds if enabled, shows 21 lines, takes x, color and pointer from the registers at the start of each line). `Seen[line]` records what was drawn; `Writes` records every register write with its line and cycle. `StealCycles` takes about 40 cycles per bad line and 2 per sprite line, which is less than a real C64 loses (VICE lost about 2,000 more cycles a frame than this model).
  - **`lib/c64/sprites.tern`**, `module sprites(COUNT: u8 = 32, FLICKER: bool = false, SCREEN: u16 = $0400, SCREEN2: u16 = $0400)`, `COUNT` 8 to 64 (assert).
    - Public: `xpos: [u16; COUNT]` (0-511), `ypos`, `frame` (sprite pointer), `color`, `flags` (`VISIBLE`, `MULTICOLOR`, `EXPAND_X`), `frames` (counts frames). Functions: `init()`, `start()` (init, `kernal_off`, installs its own handler, raster interrupt at line 0), `update()`, `wait_frame()`, `interrupt fn raster() -> bool using carry` (true at the top of the frame), callable as a part.
    - `update()` sorts by y (`sort`: asm insertion sort, stable, starting from the last order), then builds the display list into the half of the double buffer that is not shown (`build`, asm; `build_flicker`, asm). Each entry holds the registers the raster code writes: hardware sprite (entry number mod 8), x low byte, y, pointer, color, the sprite's bit for `$D010`/`$D01C`/`$D01D` or 0, and the raster line of its interrupt. The finished list waits in `pend_*` and is taken at the top of the next frame; `update()` clears `ready` first, so a list is never taken half built.
    - A sprite whose hardware sprite (the entry 8 back) started less than `GAP` = 30 lines earlier is left out. `FLICKER`: it takes the last entry's place if it has gone without being shown longer (ages counted in `update()` calls); sprites shown are stamped at the end. In a crowd of 10 the three extra sprites take turns (`With_flicker_the_sprites_in_a_crowd_take_turns`, compared with a C# model of the same algorithm).
    - Raster side: the top invocation (`next == 0`) takes a waiting list, writes `$D015` and the first eight sprites; every other invocation writes the entries that are due. An entry is due when the sprite 8 back has been drawn (`d_line` = its y + 22: the chip compares on line Y and draws Y+1 to Y+21, so a write on line Y+21 would change the old sprite's last row), not just before its own line, so a group of sprites that start together on one line is written as soon as the hardware sprites are free; with GAP 30, groups of up to 5 are drawn whole in the tests. After writing `$D012` the code checks again that the line is still ahead.
    - `put` is an `interrupt fn` (a part of `raster`) with an asm body: the private-variable rule (8.1) lets only a module's own `interrupt fn`s use its private variables from the handler side (question 48).
  - **`lib/c64/scroll.tern`**, `module scroll(SCREEN_A: u16 = $8000, SCREEN_B: u16 = $8400, COLORS: bool = false)` (asserts: 1 KB aligned, same 16 KB bank, different).
    - Functions: `set_map(m: ptr u8, w: u16)`, `set_color_map(m: ptr u8)` (COLORS), `draw(x: u16, y: u16)` (shows the map from cell (x, y), selects the VIC bank of `SCREEN_A` through `$DD00`), `move(dx: i8, dy: i8)`, `finish()` (COLORS: redraw the color RAM after a coarse step; call it straight after `wait_frame()`), `set_fine(x, y)`, `interrupt fn top()` (writes `$D016` with 38 columns, `$D011` with 24 rows and bit 7 clear, the `$D018` screen nibble, and counts `frames`), `start()` (kernal off, own handler at line 0), `wait_frame()`. Public: `cell_x`, `cell_y`, `fine_x`, `fine_y` (0-7 pixels past the cell edge), `frames`.
    - Method: while the camera moves, the hidden screen is prepared as the shown one moved by one character (`hidden[i] = shown[i + d]`, one block copy of 1000 - |d| bytes in 16 chunks of 64 bytes with an unrolled copy, 14 cycles a byte), then the new column and/or row from the map (step 17). `move()` does its share of the steps (spread over the pixels left before the coarse step, at most `MAX_STEPS` = 3 per call). At the coarse step the buffers swap (`front`), `cell` moves, and `commit()` stores the three register values under `sei`; `top()` writes them at the next frame. Eight directions: `dx`, `dy` in -1..1.
    - A diagonal move needs both axes to reach their coarse step in the same frame, so when they are not aligned it first moves only the axis that is further from its step (up to 7 frames of a straight move); the hidden screen is prepared for the diagonal step all along. A move whose coarse step comes before the hidden screen is ready (turning, or the first move after standing still) does up to 3 steps and holds the camera until it is ready (up to 6 frames).
    - COLORS: the color RAM is redrawn row by row from the color map by `finish()` (about 12,000 cycles); the example does not use it. The camera must stay inside the map (cell (0, 0) to (width - 41, height - 26)).
  - **`examples/scroller.tern`**: a 100 x 60 map built at `$4000`, screens at `$8000`/`$8400` (VIC bank 2), 24 sprites on ellipse paths (`import sprites(COUNT = 24, SCREEN = $8000, SCREEN2 = $8400)`), one program-owned handler `frame` calling `sprites.raster()` and `scroll.top()`. The camera runs through the eight directions, 128 frames each. Sprites and camera take turns (even loop: move and update the sprites, odd loop: `scroll.move`), because together they did not fit in one frame. Checked in VICE: screenshots at several times show an intact map and 24 undamaged sprites; 1500 loops took about 1515 frames (with both in every loop: 1811 frames). The example uses `FLICKER = true` and triangle-wave y paths so the sprites spread out. A video of it showed the top eight sprites missing for a few frames now and then; the cause was an extra raster interrupt (writing `$D012` with the current line raises it in VICE), fixed by acknowledging after those writes in `sprites.raster`; tops that ran after line 30 went from 31 in 1500 loops to 0. `FakeVic` now raises the interrupt on such a write too. Review fixes (first review of M11): `d_line` + 22 and `FakeVic` drawing lines Y+1 to Y+21 with a `BadWrites` check (finding 1); preparation keyed to the requested diagonal (2); unreachable `else` branches removed (3); `COLORS` caveat in the header (4); group size 5 in the comments (5).
  - Tests: `SpriteTests`, `ScrollTests`, `GraphicsExampleTests`, two more in `SignedMathTests`. The sprite tests compare, for every raster line, what `FakeVic` drew with a C# model of the sort and the build (rows of 1 to 5 sprites starting together, flags and x above 255, COUNT 8 and 64, a crowd of 12 with and without FLICKER, a handler of the program's own). The scroll tests check the whole shown screen (1000 bytes) against the map after every `move`: eight directions for 40 moves, turns, stops, a 500-move random walk, colors, the `top()` registers with other bits kept, the most expensive call. `GraphicsExampleTests` runs the example under `FakeVic` and checks, for 140 frames, every raster line against the display list the multiplexer built, one `top` per frame, the scroll registers, and the screen against the map each time the main loop waits.
- **Cycle budgets** (M11; including `jsr`/`rts` where a function is called). `update()`: 32 visible sprites, already sorted: 7,500 (233 per sprite: about 137 for the copy of one sprite into the list, about 40 for the loop, 750 for the sort); 24 sprites: 5,600; with FLICKER, 32: 8,700, 24: 6,500; 32 sprites in reverse order, first call: 19,500 (insertion sort, one-off). `sprites.sort` for 24 sprites, sorted: 750. Raster code in the example (24 sprites), including the handler's register saving and `rti`: top of the frame (8 sprites and `scroll.top`) 1,400; each other interrupt 420 on average (1.8 sprites each: 110 per sprite plus about 220 per interrupt), 9 of them a frame, so about 5,000 of the 19,656 cycles of a frame. `scroll.move(1, 0)`: 3,200 on average at one pixel per frame, 4,400 at most, 385 at the coarse step; the most expensive call in a turning run: 6,200. `draw`: about 17,000 once. A frame has about 17,000 cycles for the program on a real C64 (bad lines, sprite fetches).

- **M10, cross-platform standard library** (2026-11-02). 1078 tests pass (36 CPU, 1042 compiler). `BenchmarkTests` baseline unchanged (no handler in the benchmarks).
  - **Module parameters and `assert`** (`ModuleParameterTests`). `module name(P: type = default, ...)` header (`Parser`, `ModuleHeader`); `import name(P = value)` (`ImportDecl.Args`). `Checker.DeclareParameters` declares each parameter as a `ConstSymbol` in the module before its other constants. A value is checked in a scratch copy of the main file (re-parsed) that holds only the constants that check without an import, so it can use literals and those constants, wherever they are declared in the main file; any other name gives "'x' is not declared. A parameter value uses literals and ...". Errors: header name differs from the file, header in the main file, `module` after other lines, parameters set outside the main file, unknown or repeated parameter, no default, not an integer type or bool, value does not fit (reported at the main file's import). `assert cond, "message"` (`AssertDecl`, `Checker.CheckAssert`) is checked after the file's declarations, so it can use `sizeof`, `len` and other constants. A failed assert in a module is reported at the main file's import line, with the module's assert position added, when the condition mentions a parameter that the import set; otherwise at the assert. Comparing two constants no longer converts them to `u8` first (`sizeof(T) * 32 <= 256` was an error).
  - **`interrupt fn` parts** (`InterruptPartTests`). A call from an `interrupt fn` sets `FunctionSymbol.IsPart`; the function is then compiled like a normal one (ends with `rts`, no saving, optimized, frame allocated like a callee's). A call from anywhere else is an error. Parameters and a return value are allowed on `interrupt fn`; one that is a root (main file, or its address is used) with either is an error (`CheckInstallable`). A function that is both a part and installed is an error (one form per function; question 45). A module's private variable may be used by a part of its own module and by main-side functions of that module (`CheckPrivateGlobalsPerRoot`, `OwnPart`); a plain function called from a handler is still rule 31. Test: two module parts sharing private state with their modules' main-side functions under `irqEvery`.
  - **`cld` in handlers**: a handler starts with `cld` (before the register saving) when its code has `adc`, `sbc` or `jsr`, including a call to a part (`CodeGen.GenInterrupt`). Listing tests both ways (`Handlers_clear_decimal_mode_only_when_they_add_subtract_or_call`). `BcdTests.A_bcd_add_in_main_survives_an_interrupt_handler_that_adds_in_binary` runs `bcd.add` in main with an IRQ every 61 cycles whose handler does `+= 57`; it fails without the `cld`.
  - **Library** (signatures as built; for spec 10.1):
    - `math`: `abs8(x: i8 using A) -> u8 using A`, `abs16(x: i16 using AX) -> u16 using AX`, `muls8(a: i8 using A, b: i8 using X) -> i16`, `divs8(a: i8 using A, b: i8 using X) -> i8` (rounds towards zero; `math.remainder8` has the sign of `a`; -128 / -1 = -128), `inline asm fn` `min8`, `max8` (`u8`), `mins8`, `maxs8` (`i8`) with `(a using A, b using X) -> A`, `clamp8` / `clamps8(x using A, lo using X, hi using Y)`, `sin8(x: u8 using A) -> i8`, `cos8` (inline asm, 256-byte private table `SINE`, `round(127 * sin(2 pi i / 256))`, only included when used). Tests `SignedMathTests` (`abs8`, `muls8`, `divs8` for every input; the inline ones for every pair).
    - `mem.copy_back(dst, src, count)`: last byte first, for overlapping blocks with `dst` above `src`.
    - `random` (no header): `seed(v: u16)` (0 becomes 1), `next8() -> u8` (xorshift 3, 5, 4: values 1-255, period 255), `next16() -> u16` (xorshift 7, 9, 8: period 65535), `below(n: u8) -> u8` (high byte of `next8() * n` with `math.mul8`; not exactly uniform; 0 for `n = 0`). State is private (`state8`, `state16`), initial value 1. `RandomTests` checks both periods in the emulator and the sequences against C#; using it from main and a handler is the existing private-variable error.
    - `text`: `dec8s(dst, value: i8) -> u8`, `dec16s(dst, value: i16) -> u8` (with `-`; return the characters written), `dec8z(dst, value: u8, width)`, `dec16z(dst, value: u16, width)` (exactly `width` digits with leading zeros; the value must fit, else the digits are unspecified; nothing is written past `width`). `TextSignedTests`.
    - `bcd` (n-byte packed decimal, low byte first, pointers): `clear(p, n)`, `add(p, q, n using X)`, `sub`, `add_byte(p, n using X, v using A)` / `sub_byte` (`v` is two packed digits), `inc`, `dec`, `less(p, q, n using X) -> bool using carry`, `equal(p, q, n using Y) -> bool using carry`, `is_zero(p, n using Y) -> bool using zero`, `digits(dst, p, n)` (all 2n digits), `text(dst, p, n) -> u8` (without leading zeros; needs room for 2n at `dst`). Wraps modulo 10^(2n). All leave decimal mode off (`BcdTests`: every two-digit pair, edge values for 1-4 bytes).
    - `wide`: `struct U32 { lo: u16, hi: u16 }`, `struct U24 { lo: u16, hi: u8 }`, `remainder24`, `remainder32`. For each width W of 24 and 32, taking `ptr U{W}` (result in the first operand): `set{W}(p, v: u16)`, `copy{W}(dst, src)`, `add{W}`, `sub{W}`, `add{W}_u16(p, v)`, `sub{W}_u16`, `inc{W}`, `less{W}(p, q) -> bool using carry`, `equal{W} -> bool using carry`, `is_zero{W}(p) -> bool using zero`, `mul{W}` (low W bits), `div{W}` (remainder in `remainder{W}`), `dec{W}(dst, p) -> u8` (digits written, up to 10 / 8), `dec{W}z(dst, p, width)`, `hex{W}(dst, p)`. Decimal output doubles a copy in decimal mode (`to_bcd{W}`). `src/lib/wide.tern` was generated once from a template (the two widths differ only in byte counts); edit it by hand from now on. `WideTests` (edge and random values for both widths, against C#).
  - **Review fixes** (`docs/reviews/m10.md`, first review). 1: parts are decided from the call graph of compiled functions (`Checker.MarkParts`), not while checking bodies, so an uncompiled module handler no longer turns its callee into a part. 2: `ConstSymbol.UsedParameters` follows parameters through derived constants, so a failed assert is reported at the import. 3: the scratch copy of the main file lays out its structs first (`sizeof` works), and a constant that cannot be used says why. 7: `assert` / `module` inside a function have their own messages; the arcade comment is corrected. Not done: 4 (asm `jmp` out of a handler gets no `cld`) and 5 (inline min/max/clamp share `math.w0`-`w2`), both left for the spec session or a later milestone; 6 (cycle table for `copy_back`, the `text` signed/fixed routines, `bcd.less`/`equal`/`digits`, the other 24-bit `wide` routines, the inline helpers) not measured.
  - **Example** `examples/arcade.tern`: 32-bit score (`wide`), BCD bonus (`bcd`), sprite on a circle (`sin8`/`cos8`), random SID notes (`random`), 1579 bytes. Checked in VICE (score above 16 bits, bonus 039150 = 25 x 1566 frames). `WideTests.The_arcade_example_compiles_with_every_new_module`.

- **M9, closing the gap with assembly** (2026-11-02). 1019 tests pass (36 CPU, 983 compiler). Tern totals in `tests/benchmarks/comparison.md` (bytes / cycles, measured from `main` on): 1373 / 526134 -> **1225 / 500014** (assembly 947 / 324419). `sieve` 88 / 22030 -> 48 / 8745 (assembly 47 / 8489); `crc8` 48 / 48189 -> 41 / 37949; `interp` 329 / 7706 -> 241 / 6149. `tests/benchmarks/baseline.txt` (whole program, startup included): 1664 / 535295 -> 1516 / 509175.
  - **Variable `u8` step** (`for m in a..=255 step n`): `ForStmt.VariableStep`, `Checker.CheckVariableStep`, `CodeGen.GenFor` / `StepGeneral`. The step is evaluated once (used in place if it is a local the body does not change, else copied to a temporary). With `..=255` the loop ends on the carry: `adc` / `bcc loop` (also for constant steps now). Otherwise: add, `bcs end`, compare with the bound. Errors: signed or 16-bit step, counter not `u8`, counting down. A constant empty range with a variable step emits no code. Tests: `VariableStepTests`.
  - **`inline asm fn`**: parsed as `inline` + `asm fn`; register parameters only (a memory parameter is an error; fixed inputs and `@ addr` were already errors for asm fns); `rts`/`rti` in the body is an error; `GenInline` skips the register-parameter handling for asm functions. Flag returns and the empty body need no extra code: the call is the copied instructions and the existing branch/store of the flag. `c64.interrupts_on()` / `interrupts_off()` are `inline asm fn`. Tests: `InlineAsmTests` (also the `while` form of the empty body, which only holds for the first test).
  - **Optimizer** (`Backend/Optimizer.cs`):
    - *Same value in two registers*: after `tax`/`tay`/`txa`/`tya` a `Same(A, Y)` marker is in both register sets until either is changed, so a transfer back is removed, also around a loop (this makes `sieve`'s inner loop `sta` / `clc` / `adc` / `tay` / `bcc`, listing test in `BenchmarkTests`). The marker is never copied to a third register (that produced a wrong result once; `PointerTests` caught it).
    - *Bit test and shift* (`FoldShiftTests`): `and #$80` / `beq`, with `lda x` / `asl a` at the start of both paths and A already holding `x`, becomes `asl a` / `bcc` (`bne` -> `bcs`).
    - *Constant offset in the index* (`CodeGen.FoldIndexOffset`): `a[i - c]` is `lda a-c,x`, where `length + c <= 256`. **Not `a[i + c]`, and not `a[i - c]` in a longer array**: the u8 index wraps, and a 256-element ring buffer relies on it (question 42). `Assembler.Address` masks an address to 16 bits, since `a-c` may lie below an array placed near zero.
    - *N/Z liveness* (`NzLiveAfter`) follows `jmp` and both ways of a branch (only `beq`/`bne`/`bmi`/`bpl` read N/Z; gives up after 200 steps), so a repeated `ldx sp` before a `jmp` is removed.
    - *Asm instructions* (M8 part 1 review finding 2): they are followed like compiler-generated ones; only `jsr` and `brk` forget everything. A write to a fixed address or through a pointer still forgets all variables.
    - *Jump threading* (`ThreadJumps`): a jump or branch to a label whose code is just a `jmp` goes to that target (an `if` at the end of a `match` arm).
  - **Review fixes** (`docs/reviews/m9.md`, first review). Finding 1: `FoldShiftTests` accepts only a plain (absolute or zero-page) `lda`, not `lda a,x`. Finding 2: in a function where an asm instruction writes to a code label (`sta .smc+1`), every asm instruction forgets everything again (`Analyse`'s `selfModifying`), so self-modifying code works. Finding 3 kept: `a[i - c]` in a zero-page array below `c` is absolute-indexed (one extra cycle per access, still cheaper than `sec`/`sbc`). Finding 4: "X reused across array accesses with the same index" needed no change; listing test `An_index_register_is_loaded_once_for_several_arrays_with_the_same_index`. Finding 5: `Positions(code)` helper. Re-review finding 6: assembly that writes into another function's code (`sta g+1`) sets `FunctionSymbol.CodePatched` (Checker.AddEffects), and the optimizer then forgets everything at every asm instruction in `g` too. Finding 7: listing test for the `while f()` rule. The `while f()` rule of decision 43 has a test (`A_while_test_after_a_body_ending_in_asm_reads_that_blocks_flag`); questions 42 and 43 are settled.
  - **Not done** from "shorter `match` exits": (1) an arm that ends the loop body jumping straight to the loop test: the `match` end label is already followed directly by the test, so the jump already goes there; there is nothing to shorten. (2) A short branch for the jump table's range check: the assembler already uses one when the default arm is in range; in `interp` it is 190 bytes away. Placing the default arm first would cost a `jmp` to the end of the `match` elsewhere (net +3 bytes), so I left it. "Other benchmarks where the new features fit": none of the others has a variable step or a flag test, so only `sieve` was rewritten.

- **M8 part 2, the comparison** (2026-11-02). 921 tests pass (36 CPU, 885 compiler); `ComparisonTests` is included in that count but only does work with `TERN_COMPARE=1`.
  - The nine benchmarks exist in hand-written assembly (`tests/benchmarks/asm`, 64tass), Prog8 (`prog8`, 12.0-SNAPSHOT from `C:\prog8-stuff`) and C (`c`, cc65 in `C:\cc65`). `ComparisonTests` builds each with the external tool, runs it in the Tern emulator, checks the result with the same C# check as `BenchmarkTests` (now `Results`, so the check works for every layout), and writes `tests/benchmarks/comparison.md`. Run it with `TERN_COMPARE=1 dotnet test --filter ComparisonTests`; paths can be set with `PROG8_JAR`, `TASS64`, `CL65`. Without the variable it does nothing, so normal runs need no Java or cc65.
  - Totals (bytes / cycles), all measured from `main` on: Tern 1373 / 526134; assembly 947 / 324419; Prog8 2102 / 698705; cc65 2822 / 1453115. How bytes and cycles are counted for each is in the report. Prog8 cycles include its BSS clearing; cc65 uses `-Oirs -Cl`.
  - Generated outputs (`*.prg`, `*.lbl`, ...) go to a temp directory; `.gitignore` also covers them in `tests/benchmarks/*/`.
- **M8 part 1 review fixes** (2026-11-02). 920 tests pass (36 CPU, 884 compiler). Finding 1: `Checker.CheckLoopIndex` is now also used for arrays of structs (`e[i].hp`) and array fields through a pointer (`p.name[i]`), with error tests. Finding 3: removed the redundant `OwnHandler(first.Root, g)` test. Finding 4: the module tests share a `WithModules` helper; the `set_nmi` comment is rewrapped. Finding 2 (the optimizer forgets all state at `cli`/`sei` in asm) is left open: it needs `AsmEffects` to drive what is forgotten, and no benchmark shows a cost yet.
- **M8 part 1, the M7 follow-up** (branch `m8-benchmarks`, 2026-11-02). 917 tests pass (36 CPU, 881 compiler).
  - Private variables and the module's own handler (spec 8.1, decision 34): `Checker.CheckPrivateGlobalsPerRoot` skips a private variable when one root is an `interrupt fn` declared in the variable's own module. Tests in `InterruptTests`, including the two cases that stay errors. A handler in an imported module is a root only when a compiled function takes its `addr()`; the tests install it that way.
  - A constant-bounds `for` loop that indexes past the end (spec 3.2, decision 32): `Checker.LargestLoopValue` and `_loopMax`. The index must be the loop counter itself (`a[i]`), not an expression of it. Tests in `ErrorTests`.
  - `c64.interrupts_on()` / `interrupts_off()` are `inline fn` with an `asm { cli }` / `asm { sei }` block (`inline asm fn` is not supported yet). Listing test in `LibraryTests`. The benchmark baseline did not change.
- **M7 optimizer, first pass** (branch `m7-optimizer`, 2026-11-02). 904 tests passed at the review (36 CPU, 868 compiler). Examples checked in VICE (`structs`, `library` and `raster`; use `-limitcycles 30000000`, with 20 million VICE sometimes exits before writing the screenshot).
  - **M6 follow-up: `addr(f)`** (spec 6.5). `Checker.TakeFunctionAddress`: a `u16` (`CallExpr.FunctionAddress`), loaded as `#f.lo` / `#f.hi` (`CodeGen.Simple2`). In initial values (`data` jump tables, initialized `var`s, `@ zp` vars) the checker records `VariableSymbol.FunctionAddresses` (byte index, function) and leaves zeros; `TernCompiler.ImageBytes` emits those bytes as `LabelTable` items, the startup code as `lda #f.lo` (`LayoutIndex` follows the split layout of `[u16; n]`). Errors: `inline fn`, memory parameters, `addr(f)` inside a calculation in an initial value, in a `const`.
    - Taking an address (`addr(f)`, or `#f.lo` in assembly) adds the function to `FunctionSymbol.UsedAddresses` (not a call edge; `jsr f` in asm still is one). `Checker.OrderRoots` keeps such functions as extra roots after main and the handlers, and marks them and everything they reach `Isolated`. `Allocator.FrameOffsets` places isolated frames last, after every other frame, so they overlap nothing. A table in a module keeps its functions only when a compiled function uses the table (`UseGlobal`); the main file's tables always do. Assembly may still take the address of a function with memory parameters (an earlier decision, `AsmTests.Address_of_a_function_with_memory_parameters_is_allowed`).
    - Tests: `FunctionAddressTests` (jump tables in data, var, linear data and zero page dispatching through `jmp (vec)`; module tables; fixed routines; frames of address-taken functions and their callees overlap nothing, with a control case; 7 errors).
  - **M6 follow-up: sharing functions with no memory** (spec 8.1). Each function records the roots that reach it (`FunctionSymbol.Roots`: main and handlers; address-taken roots are not counted). `Checker.CheckSharedFunctions` runs after code generation (when temporaries exist) and rejects a function with two roots that has any frame variable, naming it: a local, a memory parameter, a register parameter kept in memory, a loop counter ("the counter of the loop at line N"), or "a hidden temporary for the code at line N" (temporaries now carry the source position). A shared function's frame offset is 0 (it has none). `CheckPrivateGlobalsPerRoot` uses `Roots` too. Tests: `InterruptTests` (`Functions_with_no_memory_may_be_shared_with_a_handler`, 6 new error cases), `InlineTests`.
  - **M6 follow-up: installing handlers** (`lib/c64`): `kernal_off()` (sei, CIA 1 and 2 interrupts off and cleared, `$01 = $35`, installs `c64.ignore_nmi`, an empty handler, at `$FFFA`), `set_irq(handler: u16 using XY)` (php/sei/plp around the two writes), `set_nmi(handler: u16 using XY)`, `interrupts_on()` (cli), `interrupts_off()` (sei). `examples/raster.tern` uses them and calls `acknowledge()` from main too. Checked in VICE. Tests: `C64LibraryTests.Interrupt_handlers_are_installed_with_the_kernal_off`, the raster test.
  - **Volatile memory** (spec 4). `VariableSymbol.IsVolatile` (on the storage of a view) is `DeclaredVolatile` (`volatile var`), `InVolatileArea` (a fixed address in `Platform.VolatileAreas`; C64: `$00-$01`, `$91`, `$A0-$A2`, `$C5-$C6`, `$CB-$CF`, `$F5-$F6`, `$0277-$0280`, `$028D-$028E`, `$D000-$DFFF`) or `UsedFromTwoRoots` (`Checker.MarkGlobalsUsedFromTwoRoots`; functions reached from an address-taken function also count as reached from one unknown root). Fixed-address RAM elsewhere is ordinary memory. `volatile` is a prefix like `noinit` (combinable with `noinit`, `linear`, `private`); errors on data, locals, twice.
    - `TernCompiler.CheckFixedAddresses`: a fixed-address variable overlapping the program and its variables, or a zero-page variable the compiler placed, is an error naming both ranges. Fixed-address variables may overlap each other.
    - Tests: `VolatileTests`.
  - **Benchmarks** (`tests/benchmarks/*.tern`, `BenchmarkTests`): sieve, bubble sort, 16-bit sums, pointer copy, small calls, struct arrays, a `match` interpreter, CRC-8, Fibonacci with a shift-add multiply. Each result is checked against C#; image bytes and cycles until main returns must equal `tests/benchmarks/baseline.txt`, so any change (worse or better) fails with a table. `TERN_UPDATE_BENCHMARKS=1 dotnet test` rewrites the baseline. Totals: 1802 bytes / 667366 cycles before the optimizer; 1728 / 623013 after redundant loads; 1664 / 535295 after loop registers (now).
  - **Optimizer** (`Backend/Optimizer.cs`, run per function from `CodeGen.GenerateFunctions`; an interrupt handler's body is optimized before its saving code is added; asm fns are skipped):
    - removes unreachable instructions (the `rts` after an endless loop);
    - a forward dataflow over the function's items (`Analyse`; states meet at labels; labels named other than by a jump or branch, e.g. `match` table targets, start unknown) tracks, per register, the set of operands it equals (immediates, `#x.lo`, bytes of non-volatile variables), and which register N/Z came from. `RemoveRedundant` removes redundant `lda`/`ldx`/`ldy`, `sta`/`stx`/`sty` and transfers, then re-analyses until nothing changes (see review fix 2). A load is removed only if N/Z already match its value or are dead (`NzLiveAfter`; `rts` reads them only for `-> bool using zero/negative`).
    - forgetting: `jsr` and any asm instruction forget everything; a write through a pointer or to a numeric address forgets all memory; a write to a callee's frame forgets all callee frames (siblings share memory); a write to a fixed-address variable forgets all fixed-address variables (they may overlap); an indexed write forgets only its array (provisional, question 32). Instructions from asm blocks are never changed.
    - Tests: `OptimizerTests`; code shapes elsewhere still hold (one got shorter: `enemies[i].hp += 1` loads X once).
  - **Register parameters** (`CodeGen.KeptRegister`): one u8 parameter passed in X or Y stays in its register (not stored, no frame byte) when the body calls nothing, has no asm block or register loop, never assigns it or takes its address, and returns nothing in that register. Also for inline functions. A parameter in A is still stored (almost every expression needs A); the redundant-load pass removes its first reload when A still holds it. Tests: `RegisterParameterTests`.
  - **Loop counters in registers** (`CodeGen.ChooseCounterRegister`): the u8 counter of an innermost loop (no loops, calls or asm in the body, step 1, address not taken) goes in a register when none is held yet: Y if every pointer access in the body is exactly `p[counter]`, else X. Not when the function returns a value in that register from inside the loop. Tests: `LoopRegisterTests`.
  - Not done (not in the M7 roadmap list): automatic inlining of small single-caller functions (spec 6.2).
  - Review fixes (`docs/reviews/m7.md`), 905 tests pass (36 CPU, 870 compiler):
    1. A parameter in Y is kept only if every pointer access in the body is exactly `p[param]` (as for loop counters); otherwise it is stored, so `p[3] = n` compiles again. In "Keep a Y parameter only when every pointer access is p[param]". Test: `RegisterParameterTests.A_parameter_in_y_is_stored_when_another_pointer_access_needs_y`.
    2. `Optimizer.RemoveRedundant` removes every redundant instruction one analysis finds (after a removed load or transfer, "N and Z already match" is not trusted for the rest of that pass). 4500 statements compile in about 0.2 s instead of 228 s. In "Remove many redundant instructions per analysis". Test: `OptimizerTests.A_long_function_is_optimized_quickly`.
    3. `c64.kernal_off` writes the NMI vector before switching the ROM out. Same commit as 2.
    4. The M7 statement walkers are queries over `CodeGen.Descendants` (`Children` lists a node's parts); `AllowsRegisterParameter` is now `KeepsIndexRegistersFree`. Same commit as 1.

- **M1 core compiler**, merged to `main` (2026-11-01).
- **M2 pointers, data and strings**, merged to `main` (2026-11-01).
- **M3 assembly blocks and KERNAL calls**, with the M2 follow-up, merged to `main` (2026-11-01).
- **M4 imports and the standard library**, with the M3 follow-up (fixed inputs), merged to `main` (2026-11-01). Details below.
- **M6 match, interrupts and function modifiers**, with the M5 follow-up, merged to `main` (2026-11-02). 816 tests passed (36 CPU, 780 compiler). Details below; the two-roots rule and the raster example's installation changed in M7.
  - **`match`** (spec 5; `MatchStmt`, `Parser.ParseMatch`, `Checker.CheckMatch`, `CodeGen.GenMatch`). On `u8` and `i8` values. One arm per line, `patterns => body`, where a body is a block or one statement; patterns are constants, `,` alternatives and inclusive ranges `lo..=hi`; `_` is the default. The checker stores each pattern as unsigned values `From`..`To` (an `i8` adds 128), so values matched twice are found and the code generator only compares bytes. The value is loaded into A once (an `i8` gets `eor #$80`, turning signed order into unsigned order).
    - **Jump table** when a compare chain would need at least 8 compares (a range needs 2) and the values span at most twice that: `sec` / `sbc #low` (if low > 0), `cmp #span` / `bcs default`, `tax`, then `lda hi,x` / `pha` / `lda lo,x` / `pha` / `rts` to the target minus 1. The tables are a new assembler item, `LabelTable`. Uses Y if X holds a loop variable.
    - **Compare chain** otherwise, in source order: `cmp #v` / `beq`; ranges at either end of the byte take one compare, others two. The last test is inverted to branch to the default (or the end), so the last arm's body follows it directly.
    - Bodies follow the dispatch, each ending in `jmp` to the end; the default arm is last. `break`/`continue` in an arm leave the enclosing loop. The statement walkers (`ContainsCall`, `AlwaysReturns`, `ContainsBreak`, `CodeGen.AssignsVar`, `Lowering`) look inside arms.
    - Tests: `MatchTests` (every byte value through chains, tables, signed values; the value evaluated once; nesting; exact code shapes; 19 error messages).
  - **`interrupt fn`** (spec 6.3, 8.1). `CodeGen.GenInterrupt` generates the body, then inserts code that saves the registers it changes (from `AsmEffects.Of` over the generated instructions; all three if it calls anything; A whenever X or Y is saved, since they go through A) and restores them before `rti`. `return` jumps to the restore code. No parameters or return value; calling it from Tern or with `jsr` is an error.
    - Roots (`Checker.OrderRoots`): `main`, every handler in the main file, and module handlers that a compiled function names in assembly (`FunctionSymbol.UsedInterrupts`: `lda #h.lo` installs a handler; it does not count as a call, unlike other functions, decisions 2026-11-01). A function with a body reachable from two roots is an error (main and a handler, or two handlers, since an NMI can interrupt an IRQ handler). Routines at a fixed address have no frame and may be shared.
    - `Allocator.FrameOffsets`: a handler's frame starts after every frame placed before it (main's tree, then earlier handlers'), so handler frames never overlap main's.
    - Emulator: `Cpu6502.Irq()` (taken only when I is clear) and `Nmi()`, with CPU tests. `Run.Program(..., irqEvery: n)` raises an IRQ n cycles after the last one was taken; the test CPU now starts with I set, as after reset.
    - Tests: `InterruptTests` (a handler with loops, calls and locals firing every 400 cycles during main's register-heavy loops changes nothing main uses; checked to fail if X is not saved; frame placement; code shapes; 12 error messages), `ModuleTests.A_module_interrupt_handler_is_kept_only_when_named`.
  - **`inline fn`** (spec 6.2; `CodeGen.GenInline`). Arguments are stored as for a call, then the body is generated in place of `jsr`; `return` jumps to the end of the copy (`ReturnJump`). The function keeps its static frame and its call-graph edges, so frame sharing, recursion and the interrupt rules are as for a call. Its temporaries use their own pool (they are in its frame). It has no code or symbol of its own; naming it in assembly is an error. `inline asm fn` is "not supported yet". Automatic inlining (6.2: "small non-recursive functions with a single caller") is left to M7. Tests: `InlineTests`.
  - **`noinit var`** (spec 4). Not zeroed at startup: in RAM such globals go after the zeroed ones (`RamPlan.NoinitGlobals`), so the cleared range shrinks; in zero page the startup code skips them. Works with `@ zp`, `linear`, `private`. An initial value, a fixed address, `data` and locals are errors. Tests: `NoinitTests` (runs over memory filled with $AA).
  - `RejectUnsupported` is gone: every keyword the parser reserves is implemented.
  - **`examples/raster.tern`**: eight color bars in the border from a VIC-II raster interrupt. KERNAL off (`$01 = $35`), handlers in the RAM vectors `$FFFE` (raster) and `$FFFA` (an empty NMI handler, so RESTORE does nothing), installed with an asm block. The handler acknowledges through an `inline fn`, picks the color with a `match` (a jump table) and sets the next raster line; main prints the frame count. Checked in VICE (`-limitcycles 20000000`): the bars and a running counter show. `C64LibraryTests.Raster_example_cycles_the_border_colors_from_its_interrupt` runs it with an IRQ every 1000 cycles and checks every border write, the vectors and the counter on screen.
  - Roadmap "done when": the raster example runs in VICE; `match` code shapes are checked (`MatchTests.Code_shapes`, `A_dense_match_uses_a_jump_table`).
  - Review fixes (`docs/reviews/m6.md`), in "Fix the M6 review findings" and "Move the OrderReachable summary back to it". 816 tests pass (36 CPU, 780 compiler).
    1. A module's private variable used by functions from two roots is an error (`Checker.CheckPrivateGlobalsPerRoot`), reported at the handler and naming both functions: `math.mul8` in main with `math.div8` in a handler share `math.w0`-`w2`. Public globals and private `data` (read-only, e.g. `text.DIGITS`) may be shared. Tests: `InterruptTests.Private_module_variables_used_from_two_roots_are_errors`, `Public_variables_and_private_data_may_be_shared_with_a_handler`.
    2. A dense `match` uses the compare chain when X and Y both hold loop variables (`MatchTests.A_dense_match_inside_loops_using_x_and_y_uses_a_compare_chain`).
    3. A `match` with no value arms always evaluates its value, so a volatile read is kept (`MatchTests.A_match_with_only_a_default_still_reads_a_volatile_value`).
    4. Copies of an inline function reuse its free temporaries (`CodeGen._inlineTemps`), so its frame does not grow per call site (`InlineTests.Copies_of_an_inline_function_share_its_temporaries`).
    5. `examples/raster.tern` explains that the bars are not stabilized and their right edge jitters.
    6. The `OrderReachable` summary is back above it.
- **M5 follow-up** (branch `m6-match`, 2026-11-01). 726 tests pass (34 CPU, 692 compiler).
  - `private` before a top-level `const`, `var`, `linear var`, `data`, `struct`, `fn` or `asm fn` (spec 2.2). The parser records the names (`ProgramSyntax.PrivateNames`); `Checker.Lookup` does not find another module's private names, and `NotDeclared` then says "'m.x' is private to the module m". This one lookup serves Tern code, types, `sizeof`, `addr` and assembly operands. `private` inside a function, twice, or before `import` is an error. In the main file it is allowed and changes nothing. `lib/math` (`w0`-`w2`, `SQUARE_*`) and `lib/text` (`DIGITS`, `POWERS`) mark their work bytes and tables private. Tests: `ModuleTests` (`Private_*`), `MathLibraryTests.Library_work_variables_and_tables_are_private`.
  - `addr(player.name)` (spec 3.4): `Checker.CheckAddrOfField` takes the field's view (a scalar or array field is already one; a nested struct field gets one) and `CodeGen.Simple2` loads the storage's address plus the offset as two immediates. Works in zero page, at fixed addresses and across modules (`addr(c64.vic.border)` is `$D020`). Fields through a pointer or in an array of structs are errors. Tests: `StructTests` (`Addr_of_a_field_*`).
  - `linear` arrays of `u16`/`i16`/pointers (spec 3.2), `linear var` and `linear data`. The checker lowers `pts[i]` to `pts[i << 1]` and `pts[c]` to `pts[2c]` (`Checker.LinearIndex`); `CodeGen.HighTable` is 1 for a linear array, so all array code works unchanged and gives the spec 11 shape (`lda i` / `asl a` / `tax` / `lda pts,x` / `lda pts+1,x`). Initial values stay interleaved (`TernCompiler.MemoryLayout`). `addr(pts)` is a `ptr u16` (or the element type). At most 128 elements; one-byte elements are an error. Tests: `LinearArrayTests`.
- **M5 structs** (branch `m5-structs`, merged to `main` 2026-11-01). 670 tests pass (34 CPU, 636 compiler).
  - `struct Name { field: type, field: type @ offset }`, fields separated by `,` or line breaks (`StructDecl`, `Parser.ParseStruct`). `Checker.Structs.cs` lays a struct out once (`StructSymbol.Fields`, `Size`): a field without `@` follows the previous one; nested structs are laid out first; a struct that contains itself, overlapping fields and duplicate names are errors. Struct names are declared before the file's constants, so types may name structs declared further down, and `ptr Enemy` inside `Enemy` works (pointer targets are not laid out). `sizeof(T)` works on structs, also qualified (`sizeof(c64.Vic)`); a type in a module is `shapes.Box` (`ParseType`).
  - **Lowering.** The checker turns every field access into plain variables, arrays and pointers (`Expr.Lowered`); `Lowering.Apply` puts the lowered forms into the tree before code generation, so the code generator has almost no struct code:
    - a field of a struct variable is a *view*: a `VariableSymbol` with `AliasOf` (the struct variable) and `AliasOffset`. `player.hp` is `lda player+2`, `player.name[i]` is `lda player+5,x`. `CodeGen.Var()` turns a view into a byte of its storage, so views need no memory and no allocation. Views are cached (`Checker._views`), so the same field is the same symbol. Fixed-address structs give views with a `FixedAddress`; since M7 a view is volatile when its storage is (`lib/c64`'s chips are in the I/O area).
    - an array of structs is stored as one table per byte of the record: byte k of record i is at `base + k * n + i` (`PlaceKind.Table`). `enemies[i].x` is an index into the view `[u8; n]` at `k * n`: `ldx i` / `lda enemies,x`. A 16-bit field is a split array whose high table is `n` further on (`VariableSymbol.HighTable`; `CodeGen.HighTable()` replaced `Type.Length`).
    - through a pointer, a field is `p[0]` with the field's offset added to Y (`IndexExpr.Offset`, `Stride`, `SecondByte`; `CodeGen.LoadPointerIndex`, `NextPointerByte`). `e.hp -= 1` is `ldy #2 / lda (e),y / sec / sbc #1 / sta (e),y`; `e.name[i]` is `lda i / clc / adc #off / tay`. A pointer field (`e.next.hp`) is copied to a zero-page temporary first, as is a pointer that is a field of a struct variable (views are never used directly as `(zp),y` pointers).
  - Struct-typed and array-field expressions carry a `StructPlace` (memory, table or pointer, plus the offset in the record) while being checked; `Checker.Unwrap` gives the lowered form where the checker pattern-matches.
  - `linear var list: [Enemy; 8]`: whole records in order; cannot be indexed; `addr(list)` is a `ptr Enemy`, stepped with `p + sizeof(Enemy)`.
  - Arrays inside a struct use the same layout as array variables: `[u16; n]` is split inside the record (low bytes, then high bytes).
  - Assembly operands may name fields of struct variables: `sta c64.vic.border`, `#c64.vic.background.hi` (`Checker.Asm.cs`).
  - `lib/c64`: `struct Vic` / `var vic @ $D000` (all registers; `sprite0_x` ... `sprite7_y`, `sprite_color: [u8; 8]`), `struct Voice`, `struct Sid` / `var sid @ $D400` (three nested voices), `struct Cia` / `var cia1 @ $DC00`, `var cia2 @ $DD00`. Unused, they add no bytes (`An_unused_import_adds_no_bytes` still passes).
  - Tests: `StructTests` (layout, struct variables, fixed addresses and volatility, pointers, linear arrays, arrays of structs, spec 3.3 code shapes, 46 error messages), `ModuleTests` (structs in modules, qualified types), `C64LibraryTests` (register addresses, fields in asm, the example), `AsmTests` (asm field errors).
  - `examples/structs.tern`: border and background colors and a sprite through `c64.vic`, and eight enemies moved 100 times both by index (`[Enemy; 8]`) and through a pointer (`linear var`). Checked in VICE (needs `-limitcycles 20000000`: loading takes most of 8 million cycles); `C64LibraryTests.Structs_example_moves_both_lists_the_same` checks the screen against the same moves in C#.
  - Roadmap "done when": the VIC-II struct at `$D000` works in VICE (example); a list of enemies updates through indexing and pointers (example, and `StructTests.A_list_of_enemies_updates_through_indexing_and_pointers`).
  - Review suggestions (`docs/reviews/m5.md`), in "Fix the M5 review suggestions": 1. `s.a()` says the field is not a function (and `v.x()` on a plain variable says `v` is a variable), instead of suggesting `import s`; this also fixes M4 suggestion A. 2. Arithmetic on a struct says "A struct is not a number", and `var x = s` says a struct cannot be used as a value. 3. `ReadsVar` compares the bytes views cover (`CodeGen.Overlap`), so `s.w = s.a + s.b` uses `s.b` directly, as with plain variables.

## M4 details

- **M3 follow-up: fixed inputs** (branch `m4-imports`, 2026-11-01). `fn get_cursor(true using carry) -> u16 using XY @ $FFF0`: a constant in place of a parameter name. Parsed into `ParamSyntax.FixedValue`, checked in `DeclareRoutine` (after the globals, so a variable gets "must be a constant"), stored in `FunctionSymbol.FixedInputs`. A, X, Y take a `u8` constant, the carry `true`/`false`; pairs and other flags are errors. Calls load fixed registers with the other register arguments (A last) and put `sec`/`clc` right before the `jsr` (spec 11 shape tested). Fixed inputs are not in `Parameters`, so callers pass only the other arguments. `-> u16 using XY`/`AY` already worked. Tests: `RoutineTests` (stand-in PLOT at `$C060`), 13 error messages.
- **M4 imports and the standard library** (branch `m4-imports`, 2026-11-01). 575 tests pass (34 CPU, 541 compiler).
  - `import name` at the top of a file (`ImportDecl`; after another declaration it is an error). `ModuleLoader` (`Semantics/Module.cs`) loads every file once, by module name, and returns them with each module before its importers (main last). Modules may import each other. Lookup: `lib/<platform>/name.tern`, `lib/name.tern`, then the importing file's folder. A module found only in another platform's folder (`lib/nes/x.tern` when compiling for the C64) is an error naming the platform.
  - The library directory is `TernCompiler.DefaultLibraryDirectory` (`lib/` next to the compiler); `Compile(..., libraryDirectory:)` overrides it. The source is in `src/lib/` (platform modules in `src/lib/c64/`); `Tern.Compiler.csproj` copies it to the output folders of the compiler, CLI and tests.
  - Qualified names: the parser knows the file's imports and joins `text.put` into one name (`NameExpr`/`CallExpr` with a dotted name), so assembly works too (`jsr util.bump`, `#c64.SCREEN.lo`). Each `Module` has its own `Scope`; `Checker.Lookup` finds `m.name` in the imported module's scope. Declaring a name equal to an imported module is an error; a module name alone gets "use the names in it as util.name".
  - The checker declares file by file (dependencies first), so a file can use imported constants in its declarations (`var buf: [u8; sizes.N]`), then checks all bodies. A module with `fn main` is an error.
  - Top-level symbols of a module are named `module.name` (`FunctionSymbol.Name`, `VariableSymbol.Name`): listing labels, symbol table, VICE labels (`.text.put`; VICE accepts dots in labels). Main-file names are unchanged.
  - Unused imports cost nothing: only reachable functions are compiled (as before), and a module's globals are kept only if a reachable function names them (`FunctionSymbol.UsedGlobals`, filled by names, `addr()` and asm operands). The main file's globals are all kept, as before.
  - `SourcePos.File`: errors in a module report its file (`bad.tern:2:5`); the CLI prints it. The listing shows module lines as `; text.tern:12: ...`.
  - Line breaks inside `( )` and `[ ]` no longer end a statement (lexer), so tables and argument lists can span lines (provisional, question 1).
  - `lib/c64`: `SCREEN`, `COLOR`, the 16 colors; `chrout` ($FFD2, keeps X, Y), `getin` ($FFE4), `cursor` and `get_cursor` (PLOT at $FFF0 with a fixed carry), all routines at a fixed address; `print(src, n)` in Tern (`lda (src),y` / `jsr $FFD2` in a `using Y` loop).
  - `lib/mem`: `copy`, `fill` in Tern (whole pages, then the rest). `lib/text`: `put`, `dec8`, `dec16`, `hex8`, `hex16`, `equal`, `find` in Tern; digits from a `screen"0123456789ABCDEF"` table. `lib/math`: `mul8`, `mul8_fast` (quarter squares, four 256-byte tables), `mul16`, `div8`, `div16` (remainders in `math.remainder8/16`), `shl8`, `shr8`, `shl16`, `shr16`, all `asm fn`.
  - Tests: `ModuleTests` (temporary folders with their own library folder), `MathLibraryTests`, `LibraryTests.cs` (mem, text, c64). `tests/Library.cs` compiles a program that imports the modules and then calls a routine directly in the emulator (registers in, memory parameters set by name), so the 8-bit routines are tested for every input in well under a second. Best and worst cycle counts are asserted in the tests.
  - `examples/library.tern`: prints a message and a number through the KERNAL, writes a label, `6 X 7 = 42` and a row of color to screen memory. Checked in VICE; `C64LibraryTests.Library_example_prints_and_writes_to_the_screen` runs it with KERNAL stand-ins.
  - Roadmap "done when": the example imports `text` and `c64` (and `math`, `mem`), verified in VICE; an unused import adds no bytes (`C64LibraryTests.An_unused_import_adds_no_bytes`, `ModuleTests.An_unused_import_adds_no_bytes`: same image and report).
  - Review fixes (`docs/reviews/m4.md`): 1 and 2 (must fix) and 3 in "Report missing imports and module name case; clearer cycle error": a missing import says to add `import x` (calls, names and asm operands); `import Util` for `util.tern` is an error, so a module cannot load twice on Windows; constants across an import cycle get an error that explains it. 4 in "Keep dots in VICE labels". 5 is question 9 below.

Including `jsr` and `rts`; from the tests.

| `math.shl16`, `shr16` | 17 + 12 per step | |
| `mem.fill` | 14762 for 1000 bytes | 13 per byte inside whole pages, 17 in the rest |
| `mem.copy` | 16777 for 1000 bytes | 16 per byte inside whole pages, 19 in the rest (+1 when a read crosses a page) |
| `text.put` | 778 for 40 bytes | 19 per byte |
| `text.dec8` | 63-290 | every value |
| `text.dec16` | 285-1521 | edge values and a spread |
| `text.hex8` | 56-58 | every value |
| `text.hex16` | 100-104 | |
| `math.muls8` | 199-252 | every input |
| `math.divs8` | 250-320 | every input, b != 0 |

| `wide.less32` | 85 | |
