### M24: float sizes and cycles

Cycles including `jsr` and `rts`, best and worst over the test values (`FloatTests.Cycle_counts`). Bytes of each routine's own code; the shared steps (`unpack_a`, `normalize`, `finish`, `store_zero`, `store_max`: 214 bytes) come once with the first routine that needs them, and `unpack_b` (32) with the first that takes two values. With every routine used: about 2,450 bytes of code and tables, and 124 bytes of variables (19 in zero page).

| Routine | Bytes | Cycles | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `add` / `sub` | 22 / 28, + 329 shared | 108-960 / 116-950 | the most after cancelling, with a long normalization |
| `mul` | 32 + 128 | 153-2490 | 153 when an operand is 0 |
| `div` | 37 + 146 | 218-4418 | 32 steps of shift-and-subtract |
| `neg` / `abs` | 14 / 9 | 22-36 / 27 | |
| `cmp` | 94 | 58-138 | |
| `is_zero` | 5 | 19 | |
| `copy` | 10 | 77 | |
| `from_u16` / `from_i16` | 19 / 36, + 34 shared | 102-509 / 106-524 | |
| `to_u16` / `round_u16` | 13 / 13, + 55 + 130 shared | 158-461 / 158-487 | `to_unsigned`, `int_part` |
| `to_i16` / `round_i16` | 13 / 13, + 76 + `int_part` | 164-469 / 164-495 | |
| `trunc` | 31 + 60 + 8 | 94-307 | `chop` and a mask table |
| `floor` | 116 + `chop` + 8 | 94-395 | |
| `dec` | 225 + 363 + 16 | 1239-7618 below 65536; up to 91101 (V1 fixes 2; was 1251-7633, 91222) | the most for 39-digit values near 1.7e38; the 16 are `text_digits.DIGITS`, shared with `text` and `wide` |
| `of` | none | none | compile time |

## Known limitations and technical debt

- Methods over 100 lines (the method-length convention; none is over 300), with the reason each stays whole: `Lexer.Tokenize` 239, of which the local `Next` is 153: one dispatch on the character, sharing the lexer's position, line and bracket count, which reads best whole (review R3, suggestion 5); `Cpu6502.Step` 160: one switch over the instructions; `InstructionSet.Nmos` 130: the opcode table; `ParsePrimaryBody` 113 and `Interpreter.Exec` 105: one switch over token or statement kinds; `CheckCall` 116, `GenCall` 116, `CheckBinary` 108, `CheckFor` 103, `ReadCopiesFromSource` 103, `GenMatch` 101: each a sequence of cases of one construct, a few lines over.
- The step loop is written three times: `Runner.Run`, the tests' `Run` and the tests' `Library` (the stand-in call and its `rts`, the chips' time). One loop for all three is accepted for after the release (review R6, suggestion 4; review R8, finding 6).
- `tern` writes in the console's code page on Windows without `--json`, so a text message quoting a non-ASCII character can show '?'; with `--json` it writes UTF-8 (R6, `b5a8785`). The name error names the code point too.
- `TernCompiler.Compile` starts a thread for each compile (`OnLargeStack`), about 0.1 ms; callers that catch `CompileError` see it rethrown with its stack trace kept.

- `OptimizerTests.A_long_function_is_optimized_quickly` (5 seconds for 4500 statements) can fail when the whole suite runs in parallel on a loaded machine; it passes alone in about 0.5 s. Seen once at the start of R1, and in about one full run in four during R2 (the R2 optimizer is about 10% slower on it, 620 ms against 560 ms from the CLI, from the jump-table check in `NzLiveAfter`; the failures come from the parallel load). A longer limit or running it alone would remove the flake.
- A 16-bit volatile `w += 256` is still `inc w+1`, which reads and writes only the high byte; it is the same question as 170.
- The optimized-against-plain comparison (R1) leaves out programs with `addr(` or `noinit`, pointer variables and runs with setup, traps, IRQs or buttons; their values may depend on the layout or timing.
- Two interface modules whose fixed routines overlap (`api1.value` and `api2.other` both at `$8000`) give "... are placed at overlapping addresses" at `main.tern:1:1`, not at either declaration (review M27 follow-up, outside the branch; for a later milestone).
- NES: `tern run` has no option for the pads (only tests script them with `NesBus.Buttons`), no dump of PPU memory but `--screen`, and models no other timing within a frame (sprite 0 hits; the length of vblank is modelled since R3).
- NES: `addr()` of a function or variable inside `chr` data would be zeros in CHR ROM (the address fill is not applied there); tile data has no use for one.
- NES: the optimizer treats a bank module's variables as it treats an overlay's (`OverlayArea` is set), so it keeps some loads and stores it could remove; they are resident RAM.
- NES: the error for `implements` on a module with no area still says "overlay" only.
- `tern run` tells banks apart by place; overlays on the C64, Atari and Apple II still share addresses in the index check and profile (a loaded overlay's code is checked as whichever overlay compiled first at that address).
- NES: the benchmarks are not compiled for the NES (not in M27's done-when).
- Apple II: the system file stays at `$2000` after the startup code copied it, so hi-res page 1 holds it until the program clears the page (`apple2.clear()`); the example and the skill say so.
- Apple II: the assembler's error for a first program area too small for the startup code says "BASIC line and startup code", as on the Atari; only the C64 has a BASIC line.
- Apple II: `apple2.plot` takes 120-136 cycles a call; there are no line, color or erase routines.
- `float.dec` takes up to about 91,000 cycles for values near the largest (39 digits), since it converts a 128-bit integer to decimal; below 65536 at most about 7,700. `float.div` (up to 4,418) could save about 1,000 cycles with its work bytes in zero page or an unrolled loop.
- `float` uses 19 bytes of zero page once any routine is used (the compiler places its small work variables there).
- Encodings: a character outside the Basic Multilingual Plane (most emoji) cannot be listed, since a character literal holds one UTF-16 unit ("exactly one character"); in a string each half has no code.
- Encodings: "a literal without a prefix on a platform with no default encoding is an error" (spec 2.1) is not built; every platform so far (the C64) has a default.
- Warnings: an unused import is reported at the module name.
- M23: after an error in a `for` or `match` header, its body is not checked (an `if`/`while` body is). An error in an `asm` block, `match` arm, struct or memory block skips the rest of it: one error each.
- M23: after an unclosed `(` or `[`, the lines of the statement that start further right than it are skipped with it (the lexer gives no line breaks inside brackets).
- M23: a function whose signature has an error is not checked; its body's errors appear once the signature is fixed.
- M23: a module that cannot be loaded is reported at each file that imports it.
- M23: `tern --json` does not say that `--max-errors` left errors out (its format is kept); the text output does.
- M22: an initial value that mixes `addr()` with compile-time code (`addr(buf) + round(1.5)`) is an error: the whole expression goes to the interpreter, which cannot take addresses. Write the offset as an int constant first.
- M22: inside an expression computed by the interpreter, typed constants lose their type (`const W: u8 = 40`, then `const K = round(W * 1.5)` is an untyped constant). The value is exact either way.
- M22: a struct field's array cannot take a compile-time array (`FieldBytes` wants a list literal); a table of structs cannot be built by a const fn (structs are not compile-time values, spec 4.1).
- M22: an asm operand outside a generator cannot call a const fn or a built-in (`lda #round(1.5)` is an error, as in any runtime code); declare a constant. Inside `emit` operands it works.

- `.gitignore` covers `examples/*.prg` (and `.lst`, `.vs`) but not the subfolder `examples/plugins/`, so building the plug-in example leaves untracked `.prg`, `.lst`, `.vs`, `.lines`, `.d64` and generated `*_api.tern` files there (`level_api.tern` is the hand-written one). `.gitignore` belongs to no session in `docs/workflow.md`; the spec session could add `examples/plugins/*` patterns.
- The compiler does not check that a host calls a binary's or plug-in's `setup` before its other entries, nor that the loaded level matches the interface it was built against (spec 12.3: the program's responsibility).

- `using X` with a call in the loop suggests removing `using X` but not why an `inline fn` does not help.
- `tern run` has no KERNAL, so a program using `c64.set_kernal_irq` can be checked only in VICE (the tests put the KERNAL's IRQ entry bytes at `$FF48`).
- The skill's outputs include addresses and cycles, so a code generator or library change that moves code fails `SkillTests` until `TERN_UPDATE_SKILL=1 dotnet test --filter SkillTests` rewrites them (read the diff: a prose claim next to an output may need changing too). Listing excerpts (```` ```lst ````) are updated by hand. Question 74 asks for this as a CLAUDE.md convention.
- `tern run` / `tern bench` (M19): memory is plain RAM, so the VIC-II, SID and CIAs are not emulated (a raster wait never ends; the cycle limit stops it). The KERNAL stand-ins (CHROUT, GETIN, PLOT, and SETNAM, SETLFS, LOAD for the build's overlay files) take no cycles; any other ROM call stops the run. The run profile names a function by its address, so two overlays' functions at the same address are counted under one name. A dumped local may not hold its last value: the compiler may keep it in a register or remove stores nobody reads.
- The source map gives an instruction to the last source line before it in the listing. Code the optimizer moves (a store sunk out of a loop) counts under the line it lands after.
- `Diagnostic.Split` finds a suggestion only after a sentence that starts with a fix verb (question 69).
- An `indexed match` on a value already held in X or Y (a loop counter), with a lowest value of 0, copies it through A (`txa` / `tay`) instead of indexing with that register (review M19, suggestion 4: 4 cycles, 2 bytes). Matches spec 11 as written.
- The run profile adds cycles by address (`Profile`), so if the runner ever loads overlays, two overlays sharing an area would share their counts (review M19, suggestion 6).

Not bugs; things to improve, mostly in M7 (optimizer).

- Register parameters in A, register pairs, and X/Y parameters of bodies that need the register are stored on entry and reloaded where A was changed.
- After the step of a memory loop counter, the counter is reloaded with `lda` right after it was stored.
- A `for` bound that is a global variable is copied to a temporary (only locals and parameters are used directly, since calls could change a global).
- Loop counters get a register automatically for innermost loops and (M18 F13) for a loop with one level of `for` loops inside; deeper nesting, a `while` inside, or a call keeps the outer counter in memory. A variable other than the counter is held in a register (F7/F11) only in a loop with no loop, call, asm or `return` inside, and only a `u8`.
- The optimizer forgets everything at labels reached from a jump table or by address and at every `jsr` or `brk` (an asm instruction only forgets what it changes); it does not track values across calls to functions it could see into.
- The register analysis tracks only equality with immediates and memory bytes; it does not fold constants (`lda #3` / `clc` / `adc #1` stays).
>>>

- Only one zero-page range per platform is used (C64: `$02-$8F`; `$FB-$FE` is unused unless a variable is placed there with `@`).
- When the value of `p[i] = v` changes Y (it reads through a pointer with another index, or calls a function), A is still saved to a temporary while Y is loaded.
- `VariableSymbol.AddressTaken` is set by `addr()` and by `#name.lo`/`#name.hi` in assembly. The M7 optimizer must not cache such variables in registers across pointer writes or asm blocks.
- `AsmEffects` are unions over the whole block (a register "read" may be one the block set itself first, as in `clc` / `adc`). Good enough for the current checks; the optimizer may want "read before written".
- Inside a `for ... using X` loop, only routines that declare `keeps X` may be called. An `asm fn` that never touches X is still rejected; `AsmEffects` would make allowing it easy (spec 6.1: the compiler may use each function's actual register use).
- `mem.fill` reloads `value` on every byte (`lda value` / `sta (dst),y`): 13 cycles per byte instead of 11. Loop-invariant loads are for the optimizer.
- A `for` bound such as `count.hi` is used in place only when `count` is a local or parameter the body does not change; a global's byte is copied to a temporary before the loop.
- Evaluation order (spec 5): parts with no call and no volatile read may still be computed in any order, e.g. an index after the value (`k[i] = k[j] + w`); that cannot be seen. A compound assignment whose index calls a function (`a[f()] += 1`) computes the index first (V1 fixes 2).
- Layout changes move code across page boundaries: M18 part 1 made `math.mul16`'s `beq` cross a page. F12 places zeroed and noinit global arrays in RAM; arrays with initial values (in the file, such as `interp`'s `code`), local arrays in frames, and branches inside routines are not placed.
- Import cycles: constants (and globals, for `len()` in a constant) are declared when first needed, in any module, but other declarations are still processed module by module: in a cycle, a declaration of the earlier module cannot name a function of the later one (`var f: u16 = addr(a.fa)`). The error says so and suggests moving it. Function bodies are not affected.
- A pointer that is a field of a struct variable (`player.next.hp`) is always copied to a zero-page temporary, even when the struct is in zero page and could be used as `(player+3),y` directly.
- A field used as a `for` bound (`for i in 0..s.count`) is copied to a temporary, like any bound that is not a plain local or parameter.
- Structs are not put in zero page by usage (step 3 of 8.2), only with `@ zp`, like arrays.
- Struct field offsets use a constant expression; since M20 follow-up 3 a constant declared below the struct works too (question 88). Globals cannot be used at all (an offset must be a constant anyway).
- A struct with `@` gaps in an array of structs costs one table (n bytes) per gap byte.
- A `match` jump table always checks the bounds (`cmp #span` / `bcs`), even when the value cannot be out of range (e.g. `band` in the raster example, which is always 0-7).
- A `match` on a value already in a register (a `using X` loop variable) copies it to A first (`txa`).
- An inline function's parameters go through its frame in memory, as for a call; register parameters are stored and reloaded.
- Calling an inline function counts as a call: it is not allowed inside a `for ... using X` loop. (Sharing one with no memory between main and a handler is allowed since M7.)
- An interrupt handler saves every register that any of its instructions changes, on every path.
- A function that is both called as a part and installed as a handler is an error: it is compiled in one form only (question 45).
- The multiplexer's raster code costs about 220 cycles per interrupt plus 110 per sprite (9 interrupts a frame with 24 sprites): grouping more sprites per interrupt, or skipping the multicolor and expand-x updates when no sprite uses them, would save about 1,000 cycles a frame. `update()` costs 233 cycles per sprite.
- The scroller copies 14 cycles a byte; an unrolled version with absolute addressing for each direction would take about 10.
- The color redraw (`COLORS = true`) is untested against the beam (the stand-in VIC does not read color RAM). It can fall behind the beam in the lower rows when combined with the multiplexer's interrupts (review finding 4); the scroll header says so.
- `src/lib/wide.tern` has the 24- and 32-bit code written out twice (generated once from a template).
- An asm `jmp` that leaves the function (a tail call into ROM, as `atari.put`'s `jmp CIOV`) adds no register effects to the block's summary: the listing says `put` changes only X. Callers are safe (the optimizer forgets every register after a `jsr`, and a register loop rejects any call), but the summary comment is wrong; it should count such a `jmp` like a `jsr`.
- `w = addr(map) - 1` (or `+`) into a 16-bit variable stores `addr(map)` and then subtracts at run time; `CodeGen.AddressBytes` could make it two `lda #` / `sta` pairs, as it already does for `.lo` / `.hi`.

- Seen while building V1 fixes: three done in V1 fixes 2; `t = q + c as u16 + 3` dropped (question 187: no single carry chain; keeping the low byte in A with `inc t+1` for each carry would save 5 cycles, an optimizer idea for later).
- V1 fixes 2: a local no instruction names keeps its memory (a run can dump it, and tests of frame placement use such locals); only temporaries and register parameters are dropped. `StoreWhereComputed` does not move a store whose index register is loaded between the reload and the store (`lda t / ldx s / sta spr_x,x`), nor a volatile store (question 186: as built, volatile accesses keep their place).
- V1 fixes 2: code that got shorter can put a loop's branch across a page, a cycle per pass (Galaxy Stars' `stars.next_busy` after gap 31). The layout does not place code to avoid it.

## Questions for the spec session

Questions 186-187 were settled on 2026-11-22 (decisions.md, `25c3ac1`): 186, a volatile access keeps its place, as built; 187, `q + c as u16 + 3` dropped from V1 fixes 2 (an optimizer idea for later).

Questions 184-185 were settled on 2026-11-22 (decisions.md, `95bc4ab`): 184 as built; 185's gaps 1 and 3 built in V1 fixes 2, gap 2 a wording change (the test suite's emulator).

Questions 182-183 were settled on 2026-11-21 (decisions.md): as built.

Questions 179-181 were settled on 2026-11-21 (decisions.md): 181's fixes are the V1 fixes milestone (built on `v1-fixes`) and its 5% threshold is in the roadmap; 180's gaps 27-29 are spec 11 rows and `keeps` on functions (built); 179, `validation/`, is the coding session's (Galaxy Stars is copied there at the end of batch 1, not done yet).

- **178 (R8, review R6 suggestion 7): the bool-to-number message for a literal.** Built: `x = b` says "Convert with 'b as u8' (0 or 1), or use 'if' to choose another number", as the roadmap gives it; for the literal `true` or `false` it says "Write 1 for true, 0 for false" instead, since converting a literal is not what a user means. Provisional. Keep the literal's own message, or use the one message everywhere ("Convert with 'as u8' ...")? The skill's example (`total = true`) now shows the literal's message.

- **177 (R6, tools 7): where a message's fix sentence starts.** Built: an error is one text, and `Diagnostic.FixStart` finds the fix by the first sentence (after ". " or "; ") that starts with a `FixVerbs` word; `--json`'s suggestion and the "(called from ...)" note both use that place. Provisional. The review asks for the suggestion not to be cut out by words; the alternative is a `CompileError(pos, problem, fix)` at each of the ~460 places that make one. Should messages carry the fix as its own part (then `FixVerbs` becomes a check of the fix's first word, not the way to find it)?

- **176 (review R5, finding 4): what a `///` comment belongs to.** Built: the `///` lines go to the next token, also across blank lines and plain `//` comments (Rust's rule), and only top-level functions, variables, constants and structs keep them (`Doc`). Spec 2 says nothing about doc comments yet. For M28: confirm the rule, and say whether a `///` before a statement, a struct field or a parameter means anything (now it is dropped silently).

- **175 (review R4, suggestion 1): idle input in `tern run`.** Since question 171, `tern run` fills all of memory with the seeded bytes before the machine's `Start`, so I/O registers and OS variables the ROM sets read pseudo-random values: the Apple II's keyboard `$C000` reads `$C6` (a key pressed), the C64's `$DC00` reads `$97` (joystick right), `$D012` reads `$18`; before, they read 0, which was also wrong (`$DC00 = 0` is every direction pressed). Question 171 speaks of memory the program never wrote, to catch reads of uninitialized RAM; input registers hold "no input" on the machine. Should each machine's `Start` set the idle values (`$C000` below `$80` on the Apple II, `$DC00`/`$DC01 = $FF` on the C64, `CH ($2FC) = $FF` on the Atari, ...), or is `--set` the way to give them? Answered (decision 2026-11-21: idle values in each `Machine.Start`) and built in R8 (`798284b`); the `SkillTests` outputs did not move after all (R8 table).

- **174 (R4, tests 12): the library tests' fake ROMs stay.** `IoTests.FakeKernal`, `AtariIoTests.FakeCio` and `Apple2IoTests.FakeMli` model a disk that can be written to, a full disk, injected failures and recorded calls, which `tern run` does not have: its stand-ins only read the build's overlay files, and a call that writes stops the run. Folding them into `tern run` would change what `tern run` does, so R4 (behavior-neutral) leaves them; the traps that only copied a stand-in are gone. Provisional. Should `tern run` model a writable disk (then the fakes become its stand-ins), or do the fakes stay as the tests' programmable disk? And where do the real-emulator runs (`TERN_REAL=1`, the finding's main fix) go: R6 (the rest of theme 7) or after the release?

The log does not check anything by itself, since only the test knows what the source reads and writes.

Open, from the encodings milestone (provisional, as built):

124. **`pub encoding`.** Spec 2.1 says "a module's `pub encoding` is used with the module's name" and "`encoding` is a word only at the start of a top-level declaration (after `pub`)"; spec 13 also mentions `pub` declarations. Tern has no `pub`: a module's top-level declarations are public unless `private` (2.2), and the parser treats `pub fn` as a word from another language. Built: an encoding is public unless `private encoding`, like other declarations. Proposed: write "a module's encoding" and "(after `private`)" in 2.1, and drop `pub` from 13.
125. **Details of the encoding declaration** the spec does not state. Built:
     - A byte may be any constant expression with a value 0-255 (`'A'..='Z' = LETTERS`), not only a literal.
     - A range is written with `..=` only; `'a'..'z'` is an error ("A range of characters includes its last one. Write '..='"), as is a range that goes down (`'Z'..='A'`).
     - `'\0'` and `'\$NN'` cannot be listed (they are bytes, not characters); `'\n'`, `'\''`, `'\\'` can.
     - A declared encoding may not have a built-in's name (`encoding screen { }` is an error), since the prefix would have two meanings.
     - Encoding names share the module's namespace with its other declarations: `var tiles` and `encoding tiles` in one file are "already declared". Since encodings are declared first, the error is at the other declaration, whichever comes first in the file.
     - `default encoding` may name a built-in encoding (`default encoding screen`), and a module's encoding (`font.tiles`).
     - An unused encoding of the main file, or a private one of a module, is warned (spec 13 lists encodings).
